Este diretÃ³rio trata de **como o trabalho de engenharia Ã© medido e revisado** (rubrica, critÃ©rios por eixo, protocolo de debate, achados de auditoria) â€” nÃ£o confundir com `docs/architecture/`, que trata do que o sistema Ã‰ (design, modelo de dados, decisÃµes de arquitetura). ## PrecedÃªncia de fontes (quando houver divergÃªncia)

Mesma regra de `docs/architecture/README.md` â€” `AGENTS.md` (raiz) sempre vence sobre conteÃºdo deste diretÃ³rio; um achado de auditoria (`reviews/full-audit-*-summary.md`) Ã© evidÃªncia de uma rodada especÃ­fica, nunca redefine os pesos/critÃ©rios de `joint-review-criteria.md` (ele mesmo linka para lÃ¡ em vez de duplicar, ver seu prÃ³prio rodapÃ© "Como adicionar um novo eixo").

## `reviews/` â€” convenÃ§Ã£o de nomenclatura e classificaÃ§Ã£o

EvidÃªncia de rodada, mesmo papel que `docs/architecture/history/` tem para decisÃµes de arquitetura: nunca normativo, sÃ³ prova de como uma nota/decisÃ£o foi alcanÃ§ada. TrÃªs geraÃ§Ãµes de conteÃºdo coexistem aqui, nesta ordem cronolÃ³gica:

1. **Checkpoints da Engineering Maturity Review** (`checkpoint-01-rubric/`, `checkpoint-02-09-consolidated/`, `checkpoint-12-redteam/`, e os arquivos soltos `_codex-*-checkpoint1-*.txt` no nÃ­vel raiz de `reviews/`) â€” produziram a rubrica CONGELADA (`01-engineering-quality-criteria.md`) e o red team que motivou G8. Prefixo `_` nos arquivos brutos Ã© intencional (agrupa antes de outros nomes em ordem alfabÃ©tica, sinaliza "transcript bruto, nÃ£o leia direto").
2. **ConvergÃªncia dos critÃ©rios por eixo** (`joint-review-criteria-round1-*`, `security-axis-criteria-round1-*`, `remaining-axes-round1-*`, `audit-areas-*`) â€” produziram `joint-review-criteria.md`.
3. **Full-audit dos 9 eixos formais** (`full-audit-round1-<eixo>-*`) â€” a rodada em andamento nesta sessÃ£o. PadrÃ£o de nomenclatura por eixo (`<eixo>` âˆˆ `arquitetura`, `qualidade`, `contexto`, `seguranca`, ...):
   - `full-audit-round1-<eixo>-claude.md`, `-claude-round2.md`, `-claude-round3.md`, ... â€” nota cega do Claude, uma por rodada.
   - `full-audit-round1-<eixo>-codex-prompt.txt` / `-codex-prompt-round2.txt`, ... â€” prompt enviado ao Codex CLI, um por rodada.
   - `full-audit-round1-<eixo>-codex-output-round1.txt`, `-round2.txt`, ... â€” saÃ­da bruta do Codex CLI. Alguns trechos que ecoam saÃ­da de outros comandos de shell continham mojibake (encoding double/triple-decoded em algumas invocaÃ§Ãµes via PowerShell) â€” revertido deterministicamente para os arquivos anteriores a cada rodada (achado real de higiene, corrigido, nÃ£o sÃ³ mitigado); a saÃ­da de uma rodada em andamento pode ainda conter mojibake atÃ© a rodada seguinte revisÃ¡-la, por ser evidÃªncia datada do que o Codex realmente viu naquele momento â€” nunca editada retroativamente. Se precisar do conteÃºdo, prefira o `-summary.md` correspondente, que jÃ¡ extrai a informaÃ§Ã£o sem o ruÃ­do de encoding.
   - `full-audit-round1-<eixo>-summary.md` â€” **o Ãºnico arquivo desta trinca que deveria ser lido diretamente.** Nota final por critÃ©rio (ambos os lados), achados corrigidos com commit real, achados restantes classificados em "impedimento externo real" vs. "escopo maior que correÃ§Ã£o pontual".

4. **Reabertura de um eixo jÃ¡ fechado** (`full-audit-round2-<eixo>-*`, mesmo padrÃ£o de nomenclatura acima, "round2" substitui "round1" no prefixo, nÃ£o sufixo `-round2.md` isolado como o de uma nota Claude dentro da mesma rodada) â€” usada quando a recomendaÃ§Ã£o de fechamento de um `full-audit-round1-<eixo>-summary.md` cita uma condiÃ§Ã£o objetiva de reabertura (ex.: "reabrir quando X for implementado") e essa condiÃ§Ã£o se materializa. Casos reais: `full-audit-round2-privacidade-*` (E-015, 2026-09-07), reaberto porque a condiÃ§Ã£o nomeada no fechamento round1 (purge worker real) foi satisfeita por D-179/D-190; `full-audit-round2-produto-multitenant-*` (E-016, 2026-09-07), reaberto pelo volume de mudanÃ§a de escopo desde round1 (Document Archive D-143â€“D-226, WhatsApp, B2B-7 a B2B-14) â€” melhora real (4,65â†’7,1/7,8), gate ainda nÃ£o atingido, nenhum vazamento cross-tenant confirmado nesta rodada; `full-audit-round2-governanca-ia-*` (E-017, 2026-09-08); `full-audit-round2-seguranca-*` (E-018, 2026-09-08); `full-audit-round2-juridico-*` (E-019, 2026-09-08), reaberto por D-231 (primeiro secret de vendor externo real, Meta Cloud API) â€” gate ainda nÃ£o atingido (5,247/5,275), convergido em 2 rodadas; `full-audit-round2-arquitetura-*` (E-021, 2026-09-08), reaberto pelo volume de mudanÃ§a de escopo desde round1 (D-084 a D-231+) â€” melhora real (7,966/8,743â†’8,55/8,782), gate ainda nÃ£o atingido, convergido em 3 rodadas, achado novo real nÃ­vel 5 (claim-before-send em `guest-credential-delivery/deliver.ts`, perda silenciosa de entrega, PENDENTE para decisÃ£o de Marcelo).

Arquivos de transcript bruto (`.txt`, prefixo `_`, ou os `full-audit-*-codex-output-*.txt`) nunca sÃ£o fonte de verdade por si sÃ³ â€” sempre prefira o `.md` de resumo/nota que os acompanha. **PolÃ­tica de proveniÃªncia/metadata**: os `.md` de nota/resumo carregam frontmatter (`status`/`owner`/`authority`, mais `Last verified` nos routers); os `.txt` brutos nÃ£o â€” exigir frontmatter em cada transcript violaria proporcionalidade (`principles.md` #1) sem ganho real, jÃ¡ que sua proveniÃªncia Ã© herdada do `.md` que os acompanha (mesmo prefixo de nome) e da posiÃ§Ã£o cronolÃ³gica descrita nesta seÃ§Ã£o. Isso Ã© uma decisÃ£o de design, nÃ£o uma lacuna: um transcript nunca Ã© lido isoladamente por convenÃ§Ã£o (ver acima), entÃ£o nÃ£o precisa carregar sua prÃ³pria metadata.

Rodada 2 (revisÃ£o). Codex R1: 6,8/10, NEEDS FIXES, 7 achados BLOQUEANTES + 3 MENORES â€” todos aceitos como reais apÃ³s verificaÃ§Ã£o direta do cÃ³digo citado abaixo. Nenhuma discordÃ¢ncia; cada achado fechado nominalmente.

## Achados aceitos e correÃ§Ãµes

**#1 (workers puros, AGENTS.md Â§7/E-007)** â€” ACEITO, confirmado (`AGENTS.md` linha 76: "workers assÃ­ncronos puros... deliberadamente observability-agnostic"). InstrumentaÃ§Ã£o MOVIDA dos 3 arquivos `src/workers/**` para os 3 handlers Lambda reais que jÃ¡ logam `outcome.kind` hoje:
- `src/runtime/aws/handlers/reminder-dispatch-handler.ts:90-91` (`logger.info("reminder-dispatch outcome", { outcome: outcome.kind })`)
- `src/runtime/aws/handlers/dispatch-outbox-relay-processor.ts:63` (mesmo padrÃ£o)
- `src/runtime/aws/handlers/guest-credential-delivery-handler.ts:55` (mesmo padrÃ£o)

`emitMetric()` Ã© chamado logo ao lado de cada `logger.info(...)` jÃ¡ existente â€” nunca dentro do worker puro, nunca uma porta nova injetada no worker (o worker continua sem saber que mÃ©tricas existem, mesmo padrÃ£o do handler jÃ¡ traduzir `outcome.kind` para log estruturado).

**#2 (nomenclatura `dispatch-outbox-relay`)** â€” ACEITO, confirmado (`relay.ts:105` retorna sÃ³ `PUBLISHED`, nunca prova entrega ao destinatÃ¡rio; componente Ã© destination-agnostic por design, `relay.ts:111`). Renomeado: mÃ©trica `OutboxPublishOutcome` (nunca `Delivery*`), dimensÃ£o `Outcome` = um dos 5 kinds reais confirmados em `relay.ts:49-53` (`PUBLISHED`/`SKIPPED_WRONG_DESTINATION`/`SKIPPED_ALREADY_PUBLISHED`/`SKIPPED_LEASE_HELD`/`FAILED`) â€” nunca um contador binÃ¡rio sucesso/falha que perderia a distinÃ§Ã£o entre skip esperado e falha real.

**#3 (taxonomia `reminder-dispatch`)** â€” ACEITO. `dispatch.ts:54-58` tem exatamente 5 kinds (`TRIGGERED`/`ALREADY_TRIGGERED`/`CANCELLED_STALE`/`SKIPPED_NOT_CLAIMED`/`ABORTED_FRESHNESS_RACE`), todos bounded/low-cardinality â€” seguros como valor de dimensÃ£o `Outcome` de `OccurrenceDispatchOutcome`. Nenhum "catch genÃ©rico de falha": o catch em `reminder-dispatch-handler.ts:91-99` sÃ³ existe para erro de infraestrutura real (schema invÃ¡lido jÃ¡ tratado Ã  parte, `schemaInvalid` flag) â€” este catch emite `OccurrenceDispatchOutcome{Outcome="HANDLER_ERROR"}` separadamente, nunca confundido com `CANCELLED_STALE`/`ABORTED_FRESHNESS_RACE` (races esperadas, nÃ£o erro).

**#4 (`guest-credential-delivery` jÃ¡ alerta desde D-233)** â€” ACEITO, confirmado (`deliver.ts:59` chama `notifyUncertainDelivery`/fila `guest-credential-delivery-failures` + alarme jÃ¡ existentes, `infra/main.tf:631-639`). Frase "nÃ£o dispara NADA observÃ¡vel" removida â€” reformulada: hoje existe alarme para o AGREGADO de `SEND_FAILED`/`SKIPPED_LEASE_ACTIVE` via essa fila dedicada, mas nenhuma mÃ©trica distingue os 9 kinds reais (`deliver.ts:94-102`: `SENT`/`ALREADY_DELIVERED`/`SKIPPED_REQUEST_NOT_FOUND`/`SKIPPED_STALE_GENERATION`/`SKIPPED_NO_RECIPIENT_EMAIL`/`SKIPPED_LEASE_ACTIVE`/`SEND_FAILED`/`SEND_UNCERTAIN_NOT_RETRIED`/`PREVIOUSLY_UNCERTAIN`) â€” a mÃ©trica nova fecha ISSO (diagnÃ³stico por outcome), nÃ£o "cria alerta onde nÃ£o havia nenhum". DimensÃ£o `Outcome` = um dos 9 kinds.

**#5 (visÃ£o por tenant precisa de Logs Insights, nÃ£o sÃ³ texto reconhecendo o trade-off)** â€” ACEITO. Dashboard ganha 2 widgets `log` (Logs Insights), um por famÃ­lia de log group (reminder-dispatch + dispatch-outbox-relay + guest-credential-delivery de um lado, o resto do outro nÃ£o Ã© necessÃ¡rio nesta rodada): `fields tenantId, outcome | filter outcome not in ["TRIGGERED","PUBLISHED","SENT","ALREADY_DELIVERED"] | stats count() by tenantId, outcome | sort count() desc | limit 20` â€” usa o `tenantId` que `logger.ts` jÃ¡ inclui no `runWithContext` de cada handler (confirmado: os 3 handlers jÃ¡ chamam `runWithContext({tenantId: ...})` antes de logar o outcome), nunca dimensÃ£o de mÃ©trica.

**#6 (mÃ³dulo de dashboard concreto)** â€” especificado abaixo, seÃ§Ã£o "Dashboard concreto".

**#7 (`emitMetric` nunca lanÃ§a â€” contrato real)** â€” ACEITO. Assinatura revisada:
```ts
export interface MetricSink { write(line: string): void; } // injetÃ¡vel, default = console.log
export function emitMetric(component: string, point: MetricPoint, sink: MetricSink = defaultSink): void {
  try {
    sink.write(JSON.stringify(buildEmfLine(component, point)));
  } catch {
    // nunca lanÃ§a - uma falha ao serializar/escrever uma mÃ©trica nÃ£o pode derrubar o
    // handler de negÃ³cio que a chama, mesmo princÃ­pio que SecureLogger jÃ¡ segue.
  }
}
```
Teste novo obrigatÃ³rio na implementaÃ§Ã£o: um sink que lanÃ§a em `write()` prova que `emitMetric` engole o erro e o processamento de negÃ³cio (`handler`) continua â€” mesma disciplina G-V3 (`definition-of-done.md`) de mutaÃ§Ã£o nomeada.

**#8 (limite EMF citado errado)** â€” CORRIGIDO: atÃ© 30 chaves em um DimensionSet e atÃ© 100 `MetricDefinition` por diretiva EMF (nÃ£o "100 valores por dimensÃ£o"). Fonte: docs.aws.amazon.com/en_en/AmazonCloudWatch/latest/monitoring/CloudWatch_Embedded_Metric_Format_Specification.html, confirmada pela verificaÃ§Ã£o do Codex.

**#9 (custo nÃ£o Ã© zero)** â€” CORRIGIDO: EMF evita a chamada sÃ­ncrona `PutMetricData` (sem latÃªncia de handler bloqueada numa API call), mas GERA custo real de ingestÃ£o/retenÃ§Ã£o de log (jÃ¡ pago hoje, mas o VOLUME cresce com cada linha de mÃ©trica nova) + custo de mÃ©trica customizada extraÃ­da (cobrada por mÃ©trica, nÃ£o por chamada). Registrado como custo real esperado, nÃ£o "marginal zero" â€” mas ainda assim baixo/proporcional: sÃ³ 3 pipelines, ~1 linha por outcome jÃ¡ logado hoje mesmo sem a mÃ©trica (o log jÃ¡ existe, sÃ³ ganha o bloco `_aws.CloudWatchMetrics` extra).

**#10 (3 componentes nÃ£o sÃ£o estÃ¡gios equivalentes)** â€” ACEITO. Nomenclatura jÃ¡ reflete os estÃ¡gios reais apÃ³s #2 (`OutboxPublishOutcome` Ã© publicaÃ§Ã£o-na-fila, nunca entrega). EstÃ¡gio de entrega final REAL de e-mail/WhatsApp (`email-delivery-handler.ts`/`whatsapp-delivery-handler.ts`, que chamam o provider de fato) permanece EXPLICITAMENTE fora do escopo desta rodada â€” nomeado aqui como gap conhecido para rodada futura, nÃ£o escondido. Esta rodada cobre: dispatch de lembrete (decisÃ£o de acionar), publicaÃ§Ã£o de outbox (fila correta), entrega de credencial guest (Ãºnico dos 3 que jÃ¡ chega ao provider real, SES). Entrega real de e-mail/WhatsApp de lembrete fica para depois.

## Dashboard concreto (`infra/modules/observability-dashboard/`)

Novo mÃ³dulo, mesmo padrÃ£o estrutural de `alert-topic`/`reminder-observability` (variables.tf/main.tf/outputs.tf, sem `tests/` obrigatÃ³rio para um Ãºnico `aws_cloudwatch_dashboard` â€” nenhum outro mÃ³dulo deste repo testa a FORMA de um dashboard JSON via `terraform test`, seria o primeiro precedente; validaÃ§Ã£o real Ã© `terraform plan`/`validate` limpos + inspeÃ§Ã£o visual pÃ³s-apply, registrado como decisÃ£o consciente de nÃ­vel de teste proporcional ao recurso).

`variables.tf`: `name_prefix`, `aws_region`, `dashboard_name` (default `"${name_prefix}-operations"`), `reminder_dispatch_function_name`/`reminder_dispatch_queue_name`, `notification_email_outbox_function_name`/`_queue_name`, `notification_whatsapp_outbox_function_name`/`_queue_name`, `guest_credential_delivery_function_name`, `table_name`, `log_group_names` (list, os 3 log groups reais dos handlers acima).

`main.tf`: um `aws_cloudwatch_dashboard` com `dashboard_body` (jsonencode), widgets:
1. **Lambda saÃºde** (metric widget, `stat=Sum`, `period=300`): `Errors`/`Invocations`/`Duration` (p99) para as 4 funÃ§Ãµes nomeadas acima (native AWS/Lambda namespace, zero instrumentaÃ§Ã£o nova).
2. **Fila saÃºde** (metric widget): `ApproximateNumberOfMessagesVisible`/`ApproximateAgeOfOldestMessage` para as 3 filas nomeadas (native AWS/SQS namespace).
3. **DynamoDB** (metric widget): `ConsumedReadCapacityUnits`/`ConsumedWriteCapacityUnits`/`ThrottledRequests` para `table_name` (native AWS/DynamoDB namespace).
4. **OccurrenceDispatchOutcome** (metric widget, custom namespace `ExpirationTracker/ReminderDispatch`, `stat=Sum`): sÃ©rie por `Outcome` (5 kinds).
5. **OutboxPublishOutcome** (metric widget, `ExpirationTracker/DispatchOutboxRelay`): sÃ©rie por `Outcome` (5 kinds).
6. **GuestCredentialDeliveryOutcome** (metric widget, `ExpirationTracker/GuestCredentialDelivery`): sÃ©rie por `Outcome` (9 kinds).
7. **Falhas por tenant â€” reminder/outbox** (log widget, Logs Insights, query acima).
8. **Falhas por tenant â€” guest credential delivery** (log widget, Logs Insights, mesma forma de query sobre o log group do handler de credential delivery).

`outputs.tf`: `dashboard_arn`, `dashboard_name`.

Wiring no root (`infra/main.tf`): mÃ³dulo instanciado apÃ³s os mÃ³dulos de Lambda/fila/tabela relevantes, passando os nomes reais jÃ¡ resolvidos (`module.reminder_dispatch.function_name`, `module.dispatch_queue.queue_name`, etc. â€” mesmos outputs que `infra/outputs.tf`'s `lambda_function_names` jÃ¡ usa, nenhum output novo necessÃ¡rio nos mÃ³dulos existentes).

## Escopo desta rodada (reafirmado, nunca escondido)

3 pipelines de decisÃ£o/roteamento (nunca entrega final de e-mail/WhatsApp â€” achado #10). Dashboard combina mÃ©tricas nativas (zero cÃ³digo) + 3 mÃ©tricas customizadas novas (taxonomia completa por outcome, nunca binÃ¡rio) + 2 widgets Logs Insights (visÃ£o por tenant real, sem dimensÃ£o de mÃ©trica cara). `emitMetric()` nunca lanÃ§a, testado com sink que lanÃ§a.

  41: export interface DispatchDeps {
  42:   store: ReminderStore;
  43:   tableName: string;
  44:   managerLookup: TenantManagerLookup;
  45:   now: () => string;
  46:   newIntentId: () => string;
  47:   newEventId: () => string;
  48:   correlationId: () => string;
  49:   /** Max allowed lag between scheduledAt and now before a claimed occurrence is considered stale (default 30 minutes - generous vs. the claim TTL, covers legitimate SQS/Lambda retry delay). */
  50:   toleranceMs?: number;
  51: }
  52: 
  53: export type DispatchOutcome =
  54:   | { kind: "TRIGGERED"; intent: NotificationIntent }
  55:   | { kind: "ALREADY_TRIGGERED" }
  56:   | { kind: "CANCELLED_STALE"; reason: string }
  57:   | { kind: "SKIPPED_NOT_CLAIMED" }
  58:   | { kind: "ABORTED_FRESHNESS_RACE" };
  59: 
  60: /** D-200/D-201: one NotificationIntent per recipient target. */
  61: type DispatchTarget = { kind: "ASSIGNEE" } | { kind: "WATCHER" | "MANAGER"; userId: string };
  62: 
  63: interface BuiltIntent {
  64:   intent: NotificationIntent;
  65:   gsi8: { GSI8PK: string; GSI8SK: string };
  66:   idempotencyRecord: Record<string, unknown>;
  67:   event: DomainEvent;
  68: }
  69: 
  70: function idempotencySuffix(target: DispatchTarget): string {
  71:   return target.kind === "ASSIGNEE" ? "ASSIGNEE" : `${target.kind}#${target.userId}`;
  72: }
  73: 
  74: function buildIntentForTarget(
  75:   deps: Pick<DispatchDeps, "tableName" | "newIntentId" | "newEventId" | "correlationId">,
  76:   common: { tenantId: string; itemId: string; occurrenceId: string; itemVersion: number; policyId: string; policyVersion: number; scheduledAt: string; requestedChannels: NotificationChannel[] },
  77:   target: DispatchTarget,
  78:   now: string,
  79: ): BuiltIntent {
  80:   const intentId = deps.newIntentId();
  81:   const intent: NotificationIntent = {
  82:     ...intentKey(common.tenantId, intentId),
  83:     entityType: "NotificationIntent",
  84:     intentId,
  85:     tenantId: common.tenantId,
  86:     kind: "EXPIRATION_REMINDER",
  87:     itemId: common.itemId,
  88:     occurrenceId: common.occurrenceId,
  89:     itemVersion: common.itemVersion,
  90:     policyId: common.policyId,
  91:     policyVersion: common.policyVersion,
  92:     scheduledAt: common.scheduledAt,
  93:     requestedChannels: common.requestedChannels,
  94:     status: "PENDING",
  95:     supersedesIntentId: null,
  96:     correctionReason: null,
  97:     version: 1,
  98:     createdAt: now,
  99:     updatedAt: now,
 100:     ...(target.kind === "ASSIGNEE" ? { targetKind: "ASSIGNEE" as const } : { targetKind: target.kind, targetUserId: target.userId }),
 101:   };
 102: 
 103:   const gsi8 = deliveryRecordGsi8Keys({
 104:     dueAtIso: deriveDeliveryRecordMaintenanceDue({ createdAt: intent.createdAt }).dueAtIso,
 105:     tenantId: intent.tenantId,
 106:     entityType: "NotificationIntent",
 107:     sk: intent.SK,
 108:   });
 109: 
 110:   // Idempotency record for NotificationIntentCreated consumers (Â§9.4: "tenantId|occurrenceId"),
 111:   // extended (D-200) with the target so N recipients from the SAME occurrence never collide
 112:   // on the same idempotency key.
 113:   const idem = buildIdempotencyKey(deps.tableName, common.tenantId, "reminder.dispatch", `${common.occurrenceId}#${idempotencySuffix(target)}`);
 114:   const idempotencyRecord = {
 115:     PK: idem.PK,
 116:     SK: idem.SK,
 117:     entityType: "IdempotencyRecord",
 118:     tenantId: common.tenantId,
 119:     operation: "reminder.dispatch",
 120:     requestHash: `${common.occurrenceId}#${idempotencySuffix(target)}`,
 121:     status: "COMPLETED",
 122:     responseRef: intentId,
 123:     expiresAt: new Date(Date.parse(now) + 7 * 24 * 60 * 60_000).toISOString(),
 124:     createdAt: now,
 125:     completedAt: now,
 126:   };
 127: 
 128:   const event: DomainEvent = {
 129:     specVersion: "1.0",
 130:     eventId: deps.newEventId(),
 131:     eventType: NOTIFICATION_INTENT_CREATED,
 132:     source: "expiration-tracker.reminder",
 133:     occurredAt: now,
 134:     correlationId: deps.correlationId(),
 135:     tenantId: common.tenantId,
 136:     actor: { type: "SYSTEM" },
 137:     aggregate: { type: "NotificationIntent", id: intentId, version: 1 },
 138:     data: {
 139:       intentId,
 140:       kind: "EXPIRATION_REMINDER",
 141:       itemId: common.itemId,
 142:       occurrenceId: common.occurrenceId,
 143:       itemVersion: common.itemVersion,
 144:       policyId: common.policyId,
 145:       policyVersion: common.policyVersion,
 146:       scheduledAt: common.scheduledAt,
 147:       requestedChannels: intent.requestedChannels,
 148:       status: "PENDING",
 149:       supersedesIntentId: null,
 150:       correctionReason: null,
 151:       ...(intent.targetKind ? { targetKind: intent.targetKind } : {}),
 152:       ...(intent.targetUserId ? { targetUserId: intent.targetUserId } : {}),
 153:     },
 154:   };
 155: 
 156:   return { intent, gsi8, idempotencyRecord, event };
 157: }
 158: 
 159: /** Looks up the occurrence by its exact key - the command carries `scheduledAt` (the SK's
 160:  * own segment, see DispatchCommand.data in producer.ts), so this is a direct GetItem, not
 161:  * the N+1 `queryByItem` + in-memory `find()` over every occurrence under the item's
 162:  * partition that used to run here (perf audit D-170: level 1-4 mechanical fix). */
 163: async function findOccurrence(
 164:   store: ReminderStore,
 165:   tenantId: string,
 166:   itemId: string,
 167:   occurrenceId: string,
 168:   scheduledAt: string,
 169: ): Promise<ReminderOccurrence | undefined> {
 170:   return store.get<ReminderOccurrence>(occurrenceKey(tenantId, itemId, scheduledAt, occurrenceId));
 171: }
 172: 
 173: export async function dispatchOccurrence(deps: DispatchDeps, command: DispatchCommand): Promise<DispatchOutcome> {
 174:   const { tenantId } = command;
 175:   const { itemId, occurrenceId, itemVersion, policyVersion, scheduledAt } = command.data;
 176: 
 177:   const occurrence = await findOccurrence(deps.store, tenantId, itemId, occurrenceId, scheduledAt);
 178:   if (!occurrence) {
 179:     return { kind: "SKIPPED_NOT_CLAIMED" };
 180:   }
 181:   if (occurrence.status === "TRIGGERED") {
 182:     return { kind: "ALREADY_TRIGGERED" };
 183:   }
 184:   if (occurrence.status !== "CLAIMED") {
 185:     return { kind: "SKIPPED_NOT_CLAIMED" };
 186:   }
 187: 
 188:   // D-170: independent reads (item, policy), fetched concurrently rather than sequentially.
 189:   const [item, policy] = await Promise.all([
 190:     deps.store.get<{ PK: string; SK: string; status: string; version: number; assigneeUserId?: string }>(itemKey(authorizedTenantIdFromPersistedEntity(command), itemId)),
 191:     deps.store.get<ReminderPolicy>(policyKey(tenantId, occurrence.policyId)),
 192:   ]);
 193: 
 194:   const toleranceMs = deps.toleranceMs ?? 30 * 60_000;
 195:   const withinTolerance = Math.abs(Date.parse(deps.now()) - Date.parse(occurrence.scheduledAt)) <= toleranceMs;
 196: 
 197:   const stale =
 198:     !item ||
 199:     item.status !== "ACTIVE" ||
 200:     item.version !== itemVersion ||
 201:     occurrence.itemVersion !== itemVersion ||
 202:     !policy ||
 203:     policy.version !== policyVersion ||
 204:     !policy.enabled ||
 205:     !withinTolerance;
 206: 
 207:   if (stale) {
 208:     const reason = !item
 209:       ? "ITEM_NOT_FOUND"
 210:       : item.status !== "ACTIVE"
 211:         ? "ITEM_NOT_ACTIVE"
 212:         : item.version !== itemVersion || occurrence.itemVersion !== itemVersion
 213:           ? "ITEM_VERSION_MISMATCH"
 214:           : !policy || !policy.enabled
 215:             ? "POLICY_GONE_OR_DISABLED"
 216:             : policy.version !== policyVersion
 217:               ? "POLICY_VERSION_MISMATCH"
 218:               : "OUT_OF_TOLERANCE";
 219: 
 220:     try {
 221:       await deps.store.transactWrite([
 222:         {
 223:           Update: buildVersionedUpdate({
 224:             tableName: deps.tableName,
 225:             key: { PK: occurrence.PK, SK: occurrence.SK },
 226:             tenantId,
 227:             expectedVersion: occurrence.version,
 228:             set: { status: "CANCELLED" },
 229:             // M3.5: the claim's WORKSTATE#CLAIMED GSI6 pointer (written by the producer)
 230:             // stops applying the moment this claim is resolved - leaving it would make the
 231:             // reconciliation job find a claim that's already been handled.
 232:             remove: ["GSI6PK", "GSI6SK"],
 233:           }),
 234:         },
 235:       ]);
 236:     } catch (err) {
 237:       // Codex Round E/F finding: mirrors D2's exact defect on this second, older
 238:       // transaction - TransactionCanceledException is not synonymous with "the occurrence's
 239:       // own condition lost" (throttling/other cancellation reasons must be retried, not
 240:       // silently treated as an already-resolved stale occurrence). Only entry 0 (this
 241:       // transaction's sole Update) failing its own ConditionalCheckFailed is provably safe
 242:       // to swallow - Round F caught that the first fix still fell through to "swallow" when
 243:       // `CancellationReasons` was absent entirely (`reasons && !occurrenceConditionFailed`
 244:       // is falsy when `reasons` is undefined); this must rethrow whenever it cannot prove
 245:       // the specific reason, exactly like the success-path catch below already does.
 246:       const reasons = (err as { CancellationReasons?: Array<{ Code?: string }> }).CancellationReasons;
 247:       const occurrenceConditionFailed = reasons?.[0]?.Code === "ConditionalCheckFailed";
 248:       if (!isTransactionCanceled(err) || !occurrenceConditionFailed) throw err;
 249:     }
 250:     return { kind: "CANCELLED_STALE", reason };
 251:   }
 252: 
 253:   const now = deps.now();
 254:   const requestedChannels = policy.channels.filter((c) => !(policy.optOutChannels ?? []).includes(c));
 255: 
 256:   // D-201 (MANAGER escalation): the trigger that materialized THIS occurrence decides the
 257:   // WHOLE audience of this dispatch - a MANAGER-audience trigger never mixes with
 258:   // ASSIGNEE/WATCHERS (they are different occurrences, from different triggers, dispatched
 259:   // independently, possibly at completely different times - estado-final-consolidado.md's
 260:   // "dedupe is scoped to the current occurrence, never cross-trigger").
 261:   const firingTrigger = policy.triggers.find((t) => t.triggerId === occurrence.triggerId);
 262: 
 263:   let targets: DispatchTarget[];
 264:   if (firingTrigger?.audience === "MANAGER") {
 265:     const managers = await deps.managerLookup.listActiveManagers(tenantId);
 266:     // Truncation is a real, named operational limit (>20 concurrent OWNER/ADMIN in one
 267:     // tenant), never silent - order is stable (whatever listActiveManagers returned) but
 268:     // arbitrary, never a priority decision about WHICH managers matter more.
 269:     targets = managers.slice(0, MAX_MANAGER_ESCALATION_RECIPIENTS).map((m) => ({ kind: "MANAGER" as const, userId: m.userId }));
 270:     // Defensive, should not happen in practice (last-OWNER protection guarantees at least one
 271:     // ACTIVE OWNER exists) - but never crash on an empty fan-out. Same auditable-cancellation
 272:     // shape the ASSIGNEE path already uses for "no candidate at all": one degenerate intent
 273:     // with an empty targetUserId, which the router's own candidateWasEmpty check (identical
 274:     // to the ASSIGNEE branch) resolves to RECIPIENT_NOT_FOUND, never a silently skipped
 275:     // occurrence.
 276:     if (targets.length === 0) targets = [{ kind: "MANAGER", userId: "" }];
 277:   } else {
 278:     // D-200 (watcher notification fan-out): the deduplicated recipient set - assignee (if any)
 279:     // plus every ACTIVE watcher, minus a watcher who is ALSO the assignee (never two intents,
 280:     // never two notifications, for the same person - the dedupe requirement research found
 281:     // convergent for this exact shape, watcher-notification-fanout-scoping/round1). Watchers
 282:     // are read from the item's own partition (same Query ItemWatchService.listWatchers
 283:     // already uses, no GSI), never trusted stale by the router later.
 284:     const watcherRows = await deps.store.queryByItem<ItemWatch>(tenantId, itemId, ITEM_WATCH_SK_PREFIX);
 285:     const watcherUserIds = new Set(watcherRows.filter((w) => w.status === "ACTIVE").map((w) => w.userId));
 286:     if (item.assigneeUserId) watcherUserIds.delete(item.assigneeUserId);
 287:     // An ASSIGNEE-kind target is ALWAYS created, even with no assigneeUserId at all - same
 288:     // pre-existing behavior (the router cancels it as RECIPIENT_NOT_FOUND, an auditable
 289:     // cancellation, never a silently skipped occurrence) this change must not regress.
 290:     targets = [{ kind: "ASSIGNEE" as const }, ...[...watcherUserIds].map((userId) => ({ kind: "WATCHER" as const, userId }))];
 291:   }
 292: 
 293:   const built = targets.map((target) =>
 294:     buildIntentForTarget(deps, { tenantId, itemId, occurrenceId, itemVersion, policyId: occurrence.policyId, policyVersion, scheduledAt: occurrence.scheduledAt, requestedChannels }, target, now),
 295:   );
 296: 
 297:   // Entries 0-3 keep the EXACT positions the error-handling catch block below checks by
 298:   // index (occurrence update at 1, item/policy fences at 2/3) - built[0]'s intent Put takes
 299:   // slot 0, same as when there was always exactly one recipient. Every additional target's
 300:   // 3 entries (intent/idempotency/outbox Put) are appended after slot 4, never checked by
 301:   // index, so the fixed-index checks below are unaffected by how many recipients exist.
 302:   const entries: TransactWriteEntry[] = [
 303:     { Put: { TableName: deps.tableName, Item: { ...built[0]!.intent, ...built[0]!.gsi8 }, ConditionExpression: "attribute_not_exists(PK) AND attribute_not_exists(SK)" } },
 304:     {
 305:       Update: buildVersionedUpdate({
 306:         tableName: deps.tableName,
 307:         key: { PK: occurrence.PK, SK: occurrence.SK },
 308:         tenantId,
 309:         expectedVersion: occurrence.version,
 310:         set: { status: "TRIGGERED" },
 311:         remove: ["GSI6PK", "GSI6SK"],
 312:       }),
 313:     },
 314:     // Freshness fence (BLOCKER-B, Codex Round B CRITICAL finding): the `stale` check above
 315:     // reads item/policy moments before this transaction commits - without re-asserting those
 316:     // exact facts atomically here, a policy disable/update or item archive/delete landing in
 317:     // that gap would still produce a real NotificationIntent for an item/policy that is no
 318:     // longer current by the time this transaction is durable. Re-asserted as ConditionChecks
 319:     // (not re-reads) so the whole transaction, including the NotificationIntent Put, is
 320:     // atomically gated on both still holding.
 321:     buildVersionConditionCheck({ tableName: deps.tableName, key: itemKey(authorizedTenantIdFromPersistedEntity(command), itemId), expectedVersion: itemVersion, extra: { status: "ACTIVE" } }),
 322:     buildVersionConditionCheck({
 323:       tableName: deps.tableName,
 324:       key: policyKey(tenantId, occurrence.policyId),
 325:       expectedVersion: policyVersion,
 326:       extra: { enabled: true },
 327:     }),
 328:   ];
 329: 
 330:   entries.push({
 331:     Put: { TableName: deps.tableName, Item: built[0]!.idempotencyRecord, ConditionExpression: "attribute_not_exists(PK) AND attribute_not_exists(SK)" },
 332:   });
 333:   appendToTransaction(entries, deps.tableName, built[0]!.event);
 334: 
 335:   for (const b of built.slice(1)) {
 336:     entries.push({ Put: { TableName: deps.tableName, Item: { ...b.intent, ...b.gsi8 }, ConditionExpression: "attribute_not_exists(PK) AND attribute_not_exists(SK)" } });
 337:     entries.push({ Put: { TableName: deps.tableName, Item: b.idempotencyRecord, ConditionExpression: "attribute_not_exists(PK) AND attribute_not_exists(SK)" } });
 338:     appendToTransaction(entries, deps.tableName, b.event);
 339:   }
 340: 
 341:   const intent = built[0]!.intent;
 342: 
 343:   try {
 344:     await deps.store.transactWrite(entries);
 345:   } catch (err) {
 346:     if (isTransactionCanceled(err)) {
 347:       // Codex Round D real defect fix: entry index alone doesn't prove which race occurred -
 348:       // ANY entry can fail (throttling, a genuine idempotency collision at index 4, an
 349:       // unrelated outbox condition at index 5), and treating every cancellation as one of
 350:       // this handler's two known-safe outcomes silently swallowed failures the SQS handler
 351:       // needs to retry (it acks every returned outcome, never just an exception - see
 352:       // reminder-dispatch-handler.ts). Only the SPECIFIC entries whose failure is provably
 353:       // safe to swallow are recognized here; everything else rethrows so the caller retries.
 354:       const reasons = (err as { CancellationReasons?: Array<{ Code?: string }> }).CancellationReasons;
 355:       const failed = (i: number) => reasons?.[i]?.Code === "ConditionalCheckFailed";
 356: 
 357:       // Entry 1: the occurrence's own CLAIMED->TRIGGERED condition already advanced -
 358:       // duplicate delivery of the same command racing a prior success on THIS occurrence.
 359:       if (failed(1)) {
 360:         return { kind: "ALREADY_TRIGGERED" };
 361:       }
 362:       // Entries 2/3: the freshness fence itself lost a race against a concurrent
 363:       // policy/item change - the occurrence is untouched (still CLAIMED), correctly
 364:       // re-evaluated by a later dispatch attempt or reconciliation, never a duplicate send.
 365:       if (failed(2) || failed(3)) {
 366:         return { kind: "ABORTED_FRESHNESS_RACE" };
 367:       }
 368:       // Any other cancellation (idempotency/outbox condition, throttling, missing
 369:       // CancellationReasons from a non-conforming store) is not provably safe to treat as a
 370:       // known no-op outcome - rethrow so the handler reports it as a batch item failure and
 371:       // SQS retries, rather than silently acknowledging an unexplained failure.
 372:       throw err;
 373:     }
 374:     throw err;
 375:   }
 376: 
 377:   return { kind: "TRIGGERED", intent };
 378: }
FILE: src/runtime/aws/handlers/dispatch-outbox-relay-processor.ts
   1: /** Pure(ish) per-batch processing logic for DispatchOutboxRelay, split out of
   2:  * dispatch-outbox-relay-handler.ts so it has NO module-level side effects (no env var
   3:  * reads, no real AWS client construction) - handler.ts modules in this codebase build real
   4:  * clients at import time, which makes them impossible to import directly in a unit test.
   5:  * This file exists specifically so m5-observability-design.md Â§5's "partial batch failure
   6:  * preserva isolamento" test (test/unit/dispatch-outbox-relay-handler.test.ts) can inject a
   7:  * fake RelayDeps/logger sink without touching AWS. */
   8: import type { DynamoDBRecord } from "aws-lambda";
   9: import { unmarshall } from "@aws-sdk/util-dynamodb";
  10: import { relayStreamRecord, type RelayDeps } from "../../../workers/dispatch-outbox-relay/relay.js";
  11: import type { OutboxRecord } from "../../../shared/outbox/outbox.js";
  12: import { defaultSchemaRegistry } from "../../../shared/contracts/schema-validator.js";
  13: import { runWithContext } from "../../../shared/observability/context.js";
  14: import type { SecureLogger } from "../../../shared/observability/logger.js";
  15: 
  16: // P2.1 (external audit 2026-09-11): this boundary used to go straight from `unmarshall` to a
  17: // bare TypeScript cast, with no runtime check that the Streams image actually has the shape
  18: // `OutboxRecord` claims - same class of gap `reminder-dispatch-handler.ts`'s
  19: // `DISPATCH_COMMAND_SCHEMA_ID` validation already closes for its own SQS boundary, mirrored
  20: // here rather than invented fresh.
  21: const OUTBOX_RECORD_SCHEMA_ID = "https://expiration-tracker/schemas/events/outbox-record.v1.json";
  22: 
  23: export async function processStreamRecords(
  24:   deps: Omit<RelayDeps, "leaseOwner">,
  25:   logger: SecureLogger,
  26:   records: DynamoDBRecord[],
  27: ): Promise<{ itemIdentifier: string }[]> {
  28:   const batchItemFailures: { itemIdentifier: string }[] = [];
  29: 
  30:   for (const record of records) {
  31:     if (record.eventName !== "INSERT" && record.eventName !== "MODIFY") continue;
  32:     const image = record.dynamodb?.NewImage;
  33:     if (!image) continue;
  34: 
  35:     try {
  36:       const raw: unknown = unmarshall(image as Record<string, never>);
  37:       // Streams captures EVERY write to this table, not just OutboxEvent rows - this filter
  38:       // stays BEFORE schema validation so every other entity type flowing through the same
  39:       // stream is skipped silently, same as always, never validated against (and never
  40:       // spamming a schema-invalid error for) a schema it was never meant to match.
  41:       if ((raw as { entityType?: unknown }).entityType !== "OutboxEvent") continue;
  42: 
  43:       const { valid, errors } = defaultSchemaRegistry.validate(OUTBOX_RECORD_SCHEMA_ID, raw);
  44:       if (!valid) {
  45:         logger.error("dispatch-outbox-relay schema-invalid OutboxEvent image", { eventID: record.eventID, errors });
  46:         batchItemFailures.push({ itemIdentifier: record.eventID ?? "" });
  47:         continue;
  48:       }
  49:       const item = raw as OutboxRecord;
  50: 
  51:       // m5-observability-design.md #2: DynamoDB Streams fallback is the record's own
  52:       // SequenceNumber, not eventId (that fallback is the sweeper's, via
  53:       // outboxRecordCorrelationId - a different source per the design's table).
  54:       const correlationId = item.correlationId ?? record.dynamodb?.SequenceNumber ?? record.eventID ?? "unknown";
  55:       await runWithContext({ correlationId }, async () => {
  56:         // try/catch stays INSIDE runWithContext so a failure log still carries this
  57:         // record's correlationId - a catch wrapping runWithContext itself would run after
  58:         // AsyncLocalStorage.run() already restored the outer (empty) context. This is also
  59:         // what proves partial batch failure preserves isolation (Â§5): record N+1 gets its
  60:         // own runWithContext call regardless of whether record N's callback threw.
  61:         try {
  62:           const outcome = await relayStreamRecord({ ...deps, leaseOwner: `relay-${record.eventID}` }, item);
  63:           logger.info("dispatch-outbox-relay outcome", { eventId: item.eventId, outcome: outcome.kind });
  64:           if (outcome.kind === "FAILED") {
  65:             batchItemFailures.push({ itemIdentifier: record.eventID ?? "" });
  66:           }
  67:         } catch (err) {
  68:           logger.error("dispatch-outbox-relay failed", { eventID: record.eventID, error: err instanceof Error ? err.message : String(err) });
  69:           batchItemFailures.push({ itemIdentifier: record.eventID ?? "" });
  70:         }
  71:       });
  72:     } catch (err) {
  73:       // Genuinely unparseable Streams image - no correlationId available at all.
  74:       logger.error("dispatch-outbox-relay failed to parse Streams image", { eventID: record.eventID, error: err instanceof Error ? err.message : String(err) });
  75:       batchItemFailures.push({ itemIdentifier: record.eventID ?? "" });
  76:     }
  77:   }
  78: 
  79:   return batchItemFailures;
  80: }
FILE: src/workers/dispatch-outbox-relay/relay.ts
   1: /**
   2:  * DispatchOutboxRelay + OutboxSweeperReminderDispatch core logic (M3.5,
   3:  * docs/architecture/m3.5-runtime-design.md "DecisÃ£o central: outbox durÃ¡vel"). Pure(ish) -
   4:  * injected store/queue/clock, no AWS SDK/Lambda runtime dependency, same pattern as
   5:  * src/workers/reminder-producer/producer.ts.
   6:  *
   7:  * Both the relay (triggered per-record by DynamoDB Streams) and the sweeper (triggered on
   8:  * a schedule, sourcing candidates via `listPendingReminderDispatch`) call the SAME
   9:  * `publishOne` - the only difference is how they discover which records to attempt.
  10:  *
  11:  * M4 (docs/architecture/m4-notification-engine-design.md Â§7.4, fechamento de rodada 1)
  12:  * generalizes this from a single hardcoded destination (SQS_REMINDER_DISPATCH_V1) to an
  13:  * explicit router keyed by `destination` - a second sweeper querying the SAME global GSI6
  14:  * partition (`RECON#OUTBOX#PENDING`) for a second destination would be redundant; the
  15:  * existing sweeper role is already privileged for GSI6 and just needs to dispatch to the
  16:  * right queue sender per record.
  17:  */
  18: import { runWithContext } from "../../shared/observability/context.js";
  19: import { outboxRecordCorrelationId, type OutboxRecord, type OutboxDestination } from "../../shared/outbox/outbox.js";
  20: import type { OutboxRelayStore } from "../../shared/outbox/relay-store.js";
  21: 
  22: export const SQS_REMINDER_DISPATCH_V1: OutboxDestination = "SQS_REMINDER_DISPATCH_V1";
  23: export const SQS_NOTIFICATION_EMAIL_V1: OutboxDestination = "SQS_NOTIFICATION_EMAIL_V1";
  24: 
  25: /** One sender per recognized destination - a record whose `destination` has no entry here
  26:  * is not this relay/sweeper's to touch (SKIPPED_WRONG_DESTINATION), same exclusivity
  27:  * discipline as the single-destination M3.5 version. `tenantId`/`eventType` (BLOCKER-B,
  28:  * reminder-delivery-pipeline.md Â§4) are the record's own envelope fields, passed through so
  29:  * a sender whose payload doesn't already self-describe them (unlike DispatchCommand, which
  30:  * embeds its own tenantId/commandType) can still build a self-contained SQS message -
  31:  * existing senders (dispatch/email/chasing/import, whose payloads already carry everything
  32:  * they need) simply ignore the two extra arguments. */
  33: export type DestinationSenders = Partial<
  34:   Record<OutboxDestination, (payload: Record<string, unknown>, correlationId: string, tenantId: string, eventType: string) => Promise<void>>
  35: >;
  36: 
  37: export interface RelayDeps {
  38:   store: OutboxRelayStore;
  39:   senders: DestinationSenders;
  40:   now: () => string;
  41:   leaseOwner: string;
  42:   /** How long a lease is held while attempting SendMessage - default 30s, comfortably
  43:    * longer than one SQS SendMessage call, short enough that a crashed relay invocation's
  44:    * lease is quickly reclaimable by the next relay/sweeper attempt. */
  45:   leaseDurationMs?: number;
  46: }
  47: 
  48: export type PublishOutcome =
  49:   | { kind: "PUBLISHED" }
  50:   | { kind: "SKIPPED_WRONG_DESTINATION" }
  51:   | { kind: "SKIPPED_ALREADY_PUBLISHED" }
  52:   | { kind: "SKIPPED_LEASE_HELD" }
  53:   | { kind: "FAILED"; error: unknown };
  54: 
  55: /** Attempts to publish exactly one outbox record. Never throws for expected outcomes
  56:  * (wrong destination / already published / lease contention) - only for genuinely
  57:  * unexpected failures during acquire/send/mark, surfaced as `FAILED` so the caller (relay
  58:  * Lambda: partial batch failure; sweeper: per-item log+continue) decides retry policy. */
  59: export async function publishOne(deps: RelayDeps, record: OutboxRecord): Promise<PublishOutcome> {
  60:   // Exclusivity (docs/architecture/m3.5-runtime-design.md Â§"DecisÃ£o central"): this relay/
  61:   // sweeper NEVER touches a record it doesn't own the destination for - OutboxPublisher
  62:   // (generic, EventBridge) owns everything else, including records with no `destination`.
  63:   const send = record.destination ? deps.senders[record.destination] : undefined;
  64:   if (!send) {
  65:     return { kind: "SKIPPED_WRONG_DESTINATION" };
  66:   }
  67:   if (record.status === "PUBLISHED") {
  68:     return { kind: "SKIPPED_ALREADY_PUBLISHED" };
  69:   }
  70: 
  71:   // m5-observability-design.md #2: one runWithContext per record - correlationId read from
  72:   // the persisted OutboxRecord (the business operation that originally created it), never
  73:   // from this invocation's own ambient context.
  74:   return runWithContext({ correlationId: outboxRecordCorrelationId(record) }, () => publishAcquiredOrPending(deps, record, send));
  75: }
  76: 
  77: async function publishAcquiredOrPending(
  78:   deps: RelayDeps,
  79:   record: OutboxRecord,
  80:   send: (payload: Record<string, unknown>, correlationId: string, tenantId: string, eventType: string) => Promise<void>,
  81: ): Promise<PublishOutcome> {
  82:   const leaseDurationMs = deps.leaseDurationMs ?? 30_000;
  83:   const now = deps.now();
  84:   const leaseExpiresAt = new Date(Date.parse(now) + leaseDurationMs).toISOString();
  85: 
  86:   const acquired = await deps.store.tryAcquireLease({ PK: record.PK, SK: record.SK }, deps.leaseOwner, leaseExpiresAt, now);
  87:   if (!acquired) {
  88:     return { kind: "SKIPPED_LEASE_HELD" };
  89:   }
  90: 
  91:   try {
  92:     await send(record.payload, outboxRecordCorrelationId(record), record.tenantId, record.eventType);
  93:   } catch (error) {
  94:     // Failure here is expected and recoverable: the lease expires, the sweeper (or a later
  95:     // Stream retry) will retry. A duplicate SendMessage after a retry is absorbed by the
  96:     // downstream worker's own idempotency - never treated as a reason to skip retrying.
  97:     return { kind: "FAILED", error };
  98:   }
  99: 
 100:   // Transition PENDING -> PUBLISHED only AFTER SendMessage confirmed (m3.5-runtime-design.md:
 101:   // "falha entre (2) e (3) Ã© aceitÃ¡vel - duplicata Ã© absorvida pela idempotÃªncia do
 102:   // dispatch"). Failure here also just means the sweeper resends a message that was
 103:   // already delivered once - still recoverable, not a correctness issue.
 104:   await deps.store.markPublished({ PK: record.PK, SK: record.SK });
 105:   return { kind: "PUBLISHED" };
 106: }
 107: 
 108: /** DispatchOutboxRelay / NotificationEmailOutboxRelay entrypoint logic: one call per
 109:  * DynamoDB Streams record (NEW_IMAGE). Which record types actually flow through a given
 110:  * Lambda's Streams filter, and thus which `senders` it needs, is a wiring concern, not this
 111:  * function's - it stays destination-agnostic. */
 112: export async function relayStreamRecord(deps: RelayDeps, record: OutboxRecord): Promise<PublishOutcome> {
 113:   return publishOne(deps, record);
 114: }
 115: 
 116: export interface SweeperDeps extends RelayDeps {
 117:   /** Minimum age (ms) before a PENDING record is swept - covers relay/Stream failure, not
 118:    * normal in-flight latency (m3.5-runtime-design.md: "occurredAt mais antigo que um
 119:    * limiar (2 min)"). */
 120:   minAgeMs?: number;
 121:   pageSize?: number;
 122:   /** Which destinations to sweep this run - the sweeper queries GSI6 once per destination
 123:    * (same global partition, `RECON#OUTBOX#PENDING`, filtered by `destination` server-side by
 124:    * the store). Defaults to every destination this deps' `senders` map recognizes. */
 125:   destinations?: OutboxDestination[];
 126: }
 127: 
 128: export interface SweepResult {
 129:   attempted: number;
 130:   published: number;
 131:   failed: number;
 132:   stillPending: number;
 133: }
 134: 
 135: /** OutboxSweeperReminderDispatch/OutboxSweeperNotificationEmail entrypoint logic: no backoff
 136:  * of its own - every scheduled execution IS one retry attempt (m3.5-runtime-design.md:
 137:  * "nenhum backoff prÃ³prio"). One sweeper instance can cover multiple destinations (M4
 138:  * Â§7.4: "nÃ£o proponho outro GSI nem scan... o sweeper existente deve evoluir para um
 139:  * roteador explÃ­cito por destination"). */
 140: export async function sweepPendingDispatch(deps: SweeperDeps): Promise<SweepResult> {
 141:   const minAgeMs = deps.minAgeMs ?? 2 * 60_000;
 142:   const olderThan = new Date(Date.parse(deps.now()) - minAgeMs).toISOString();
 143:   const destinations = deps.destinations ?? (Object.keys(deps.senders) as OutboxDestination[]);
 144: 
 145:   let attempted = 0;
 146:   let published = 0;
 147:   let failed = 0;
 148:   let stillPending = 0;
 149: 
 150:   for (const destination of destinations) {
 151:     const candidates = await deps.store.listPendingReminderDispatch({
 152:       destination,
 153:       olderThan,
 154:       pageSize: deps.pageSize,
 155:     });
 156: 
 157:     attempted += candidates.length;
 158:     for (const record of candidates) {
 159:       const outcome = await publishOne(deps, record);
 160:       if (outcome.kind === "PUBLISHED") published += 1;
 161:       else if (outcome.kind === "FAILED") failed += 1;
 162:       else stillPending += 1;
 163:     }
 164:   }
 165: 
 166:   return { attempted, published, failed, stillPending };
 167: }

   1: /** Real handler for GuestCredentialDelivery (DynamoDB Streams NEW_IMAGE off the DEDICATED
   2:  * `guest-credential-delivery` table, D-226 decision central item 5 â€” never the main table's
   3:  * own outbox relay). D-228 closes the gap D-222/D-227 named: this worker did not exist before.
   4:  * Event Source Mapping filters `eventName = INSERT` in Terraform (`infra/main.tf`) â€” the

   7:  * entrypoint only â€” real logic lives in `workers/guest-credential-delivery/deliver.ts`. */
   8: import type { DynamoDBBatchResponse, DynamoDBStreamEvent } from "aws-lambda";

  17: const client = createDocumentClient();

  19: const deliveryTableName = process.env["GUEST_CREDENTIAL_DELIVERY_TABLE_NAME"];
  20: const sesFromAddress = process.env["SES_FROM_ADDRESS"];
  21: const sesConfigurationSet = process.env["SES_CONFIGURATION_SET"];
  22: const guestUploadBaseUrl = process.env["GUEST_UPLOAD_BASE_URL"];
  23: const failuresQueueUrl = process.env["GUEST_CREDENTIAL_DELIVERY_FAILURES_QUEUE_URL"];
  24: if (!tableName) throw new Error("TABLE_NAME env var is required.");
  25: if (!deliveryTableName) throw new Error("GUEST_CREDENTIAL_DELIVERY_TABLE_NAME env var is required.");
  26: if (!sesFromAddress) throw new Error("SES_FROM_ADDRESS env var is required.");
  27: if (!sesConfigurationSet) throw new Error("SES_CONFIGURATION_SET env var is required.");

  29: const deps = buildGuestCredentialDeliveryDeps(client, tableName, deliveryTableName, sesFromAddress, sesConfigurationSet, failuresQueueUrl, guestUploadBaseUrl);
  30: const logger = new SecureLogger({ baseContext: { service: "guest-credential-delivery" } });
  31: 
  32: function isDeliveryRecord(value: unknown): value is GuestCredentialDeliveryRecord {
  33:   const v = value as Partial<GuestCredentialDeliveryRecord> | undefined;
  34:   return typeof v?.tenantId === "string" && typeof v?.subjectId === "string" && typeof v?.documentRequestId === "string" && typeof v?.issuanceGeneration === "number" && typeof v?.token === "string";
  35: }
  36: 
  37: export async function handler(event: DynamoDBStreamEvent): Promise<DynamoDBBatchResponse> {
  38:   const batchItemFailures: { itemIdentifier: string }[] = [];
  39: 
  40:   for (const streamRecord of event.Records) {
  41:     if (streamRecord.eventName !== "INSERT") continue;
  42:     const image = streamRecord.dynamodb?.NewImage;
  43:     if (!image) continue;
  44: 
  45:     await runWithContext({ correlationId: streamRecord.dynamodb?.SequenceNumber ?? streamRecord.eventID ?? "unknown" }, async () => {
  46:       try {
  47:         const record = unmarshall(image as Record<string, never>) as unknown;
  48:         if (!isDeliveryRecord(record) || record.entityType !== "GuestCredentialDelivery") {
  49:           logger.error("guest-credential-delivery malformed Streams image", { eventID: streamRecord.eventID });
  50:           batchItemFailures.push({ itemIdentifier: streamRecord.eventID ?? "" });
  51:           return;
  52:         }
  53:         await runWithContext({ correlationId: streamRecord.eventID ?? "unknown", tenantId: record.tenantId }, async () => {
  54:           const outcome = await deliverGuestCredential(deps, record);
  55:           logger.info("guest-credential-delivery outcome", { documentRequestId: record.documentRequestId, outcome: outcome.kind });
  56:           // D-233: SKIPPED_LEASE_ACTIVE must be retried like SEND_FAILED, never treated as a
  57:           // completed/successful invocation - it's how a redelivery keeps happening until the
  58:           // claim's lease expires and reconciles (see deliver.ts's header comment).
  59:           if (outcome.kind === "SEND_FAILED" || outcome.kind === "SKIPPED_LEASE_ACTIVE") {
  60:             batchItemFailures.push({ itemIdentifier: streamRecord.eventID ?? "" });
  61:           }
  62:         });
  63:       } catch (err) {
  64:         logger.error("guest-credential-delivery failed", { eventID: streamRecord.eventID, error: err instanceof Error ? err.message : String(err) });
  65:         batchItemFailures.push({ itemIdentifier: streamRecord.eventID ?? "" });
  66:       }
  67:     });
  68:   }
  69: 
  70:   return { batchItemFailures };
  71: }
FILE: src/workers/guest-credential-delivery/deliver.ts
   1: /**
   2:  * GuestCredentialDeliveryWorker â€” D-228 (closes D-222 Achado 1 for real), revised by D-233
   3:  * (fixes SEC-R2-02/full-audit-round2, both SeguranÃ§a and Arquitetura axes converged on the
   4:  * same finding independently): the original `deliver.ts` claimed the delivery marker BEFORE
   5:  * calling `emailProvider.send()` with no way back â€” a transient send failure (SES throttled,
   6:  * momentarily unavailable) permanently and silently lost the guest's only credential link,
   7:  * because a Streams retry found the marker already claimed and returned `ALREADY_DELIVERED`
   8:  * instead of resending. Full mechanism/decision record: `docs/architecture/decisions-log.md`
   9:  * D-233; Claude<->Codex convergence: `docs/engineering/reviews/d-233-*` (8 rounds, both â‰¥9.0).
  10:  *
  11:  * Consumes ONE `GuestCredentialDeliveryRecord` (already unmarshalled from the dedicated table's
  12:  * own DynamoDB Streams NEW_IMAGE, INSERT only â€” the record is created exactly once per issuance,
  13:  * never updated) and, if a recipient is known, emails the guest link that actually authenticates
  14:  * via `resolveCredential()`.
  15:  *
  16:  * Deliberately re-reads the authoritative `DocumentRequest` (main table) rather than trusting
  17:  * anything beyond the record's own key fields â€” same discipline
  18:  * `DocumentRequestCredentialIssuanceService.handle` already documents for the producer side.
  19:  * Two independent staleness/eligibility guards before ever sending:
  20:  *  - `request.issuanceGeneration !== record.issuanceGeneration`: a newer reissuance
  21:  *    (`rejectVersion()`) has already superseded this delivery â€” safe no-op.
  22:  *  - `!request.recipientEmail`: a genuine data gap (D-228 pendency â€” series-materialized
  23:  *    requests carry no recipient contact yet), terminal and NOT retried; retrying can never
  24:  *    make an absent field appear.
  25:  *
  26:  * Idempotency under Streams' at-least-once redelivery (G-V3 requirement), REVISED mechanism
  27:  * (D-233): a lease-based claim, proportional variant of the same PRINCIPLE
  28:  * `NotificationAttempt`/`email-delivery-workflow.ts` already established for this exact problem
  29:  * class (never `NotificationAttempt`'s full OCC/version machinery â€” a guest link is a static,
  30:  * harmlessly re-sendable value, unlike a customer-facing paid send).
  31:  *  - `markerStore.claim()` returns `CLAIMED` (safe to send), `LEASE_ACTIVE` (another invocation
  32:  *    is sending right now or died very recently â€” retryable, never a silent skip),
  33:  *    `ALREADY_DELIVERED` (terminal, confirmed sent), or `PREVIOUSLY_UNCERTAIN` (a prior attempt
  34:  *    could not conclusively rule out having sent â€” terminal for automated resend, alerted).
  35:  *  - `EmailProviderAdapter`'s existing `EmailSendFailureKind` taxonomy
  36:  *    (`CONCLUSIVE_RETRYABLE`/`CONCLUSIVE_TERMINAL`/`AMBIGUOUS`, `email-provider.ts`) decides the
  37:  *    outcome: ONLY `CONCLUSIVE_RETRYABLE` (SES definitively rejected the request before
  38:  *    accepting it) releases the claim for an immediate, safe automatic retry. Every other
  39:  *    outcome (`AMBIGUOUS`, `CONCLUSIVE_TERMINAL`, or the send succeeding but `markDelivered`
  40:  *    itself failing to persist) transitions to `SEND_UNCERTAIN` â€” SES may have already accepted
  41:  *    the message, so a duplicate send would be a real, avoidable failure â€” paired ALWAYS with
  42:  *    `notifyUncertainDelivery` (an alert into the existing `guest-credential-delivery-failures`
  43:  *    queue/alarm, never silent, never carrying the raw token).
  44:  *  - A claim whose lease expires without ANY of the above transitions (process died at an
  45:  *    indeterminate point, including possibly after SES already accepted) is reconciled by
  46:  *    `claim()` ITSELF into `SEND_UNCERTAIN` on the next invocation â€” never resent automatically.
  47:  *
  48:  * The raw token (`record.token`) is embedded in the email body â€” its ONLY appearance outside
  49:  * the dedicated delivery table â€” and never logged, never written to any other row (the alert
  50:  * payload for SEND_UNCERTAIN carries only key/metadata fields, never the token).
  51:  */
  52: import { EmailSendError, type EmailSendFailureKind, type EmailProviderAdapter } from "../../modules/notification/ports/email-provider.js";
  53: import type { GuestCredentialDeliveryMarkerStore } from "../../modules/document-archive/ports/guest-credential-delivery-marker-store.js";
  54: import { documentRequestKey, type DocumentRequest } from "../../modules/document-archive/domain/document-request.js";
  55: import type { GuestCredentialDeliveryRecord } from "../../modules/document-archive/domain/guest-credential-delivery.js";
  56: import { authorizedTenantIdFromPersistedEntity } from "../../modules/identity/domain/authorization.js";
  57: 
  58: export interface GuestCredentialDeliveryStore {
  59:   get<T extends { PK: string; SK: string } = DocumentRequest & { PK: string; SK: string }>(key: { PK: string; SK: string }): Promise<T | undefined>;
  60: }
  61: 
  62: export interface UncertainDeliveryAlert {
  63:   documentRequestId: string;
  64:   issuanceGeneration: number;
  65:   failureKind: string;
  66:   correlationId: string;
  67: }
  68: 
  69: export interface GuestCredentialDeliveryDeps {
  70:   store: GuestCredentialDeliveryStore;
  71:   markerStore: GuestCredentialDeliveryMarkerStore;
  72:   emailProvider: EmailProviderAdapter;
  73:   /** Sends an operator-facing alert (metadata only, never the token) when a delivery could not
  74:    * be conclusively resolved as sent or not-sent â€” real implementation is an SQS SendMessage
  75:    * into the already-existing `guest-credential-delivery-failures` queue (composition root),
  76:    * kept as an injected dependency so this worker stays AWS-SDK-free (AGENTS.md Â§7). */
  77:   notifyUncertainDelivery: (alert: UncertainDeliveryAlert) => Promise<void>;
  78:   /** Base URL of G02's real route (`docs/frontend/prototype-screen-specs/
  79:    * G02-solicitacao-documento-convidado.md`'s own route contract:
  80:    * `/document-archive/guest/document-requests/:token`, a PATH segment â€” never `?token=`, the
  81:    * placeholder shape this deliberately superseded once the frontend route existed to conform
  82:    * to, Block 6/D-2xx). No trailing slash â€” `deliverGuestCredential` appends `/${token}`. */
  83:   guestUploadBaseUrl: string;
  84:   now: () => string;
  85:   newCorrelationId: () => string;
  86:   /** How long a claim is leased before an unresolved invocation is treated as died-mid-flight
  87:    * (D-233 round 5/6: kept short â€” 30s default â€” because every OBSERVABLE outcome already
  88:    * resolves synchronously via releaseClaim/markDelivered/markUncertain; the lease only covers
  89:    * the residual case of a process dying with no observable outcome at all). */
  90:   leaseDurationMs?: number;
  91: }
  92: 
  93: export type GuestCredentialDeliveryOutcome =
  94:   | { kind: "SENT" }
  95:   | { kind: "ALREADY_DELIVERED" }
  96:   | { kind: "SKIPPED_REQUEST_NOT_FOUND" }
  97:   | { kind: "SKIPPED_STALE_GENERATION" }
  98:   | { kind: "SKIPPED_NO_RECIPIENT_EMAIL" }
  99:   | { kind: "SKIPPED_LEASE_ACTIVE" }
 100:   | { kind: "SEND_FAILED"; error: string }
 101:   | { kind: "SEND_UNCERTAIN_NOT_RETRIED"; failureKind: string }
 102:   | { kind: "PREVIOUSLY_UNCERTAIN"; failureKind: string };
 103: 
 104: export async function deliverGuestCredential(deps: GuestCredentialDeliveryDeps, record: GuestCredentialDeliveryRecord): Promise<GuestCredentialDeliveryOutcome> {
 105:   const request = await deps.store.get<DocumentRequest>(documentRequestKey(authorizedTenantIdFromPersistedEntity(record), record.subjectId, record.documentRequestId));
 106:   if (!request) return { kind: "SKIPPED_REQUEST_NOT_FOUND" };
 107:   if (request.issuanceGeneration !== record.issuanceGeneration) return { kind: "SKIPPED_STALE_GENERATION" };
 108:   if (!request.recipientEmail) return { kind: "SKIPPED_NO_RECIPIENT_EMAIL" };
 109: 
 110:   const now = deps.now();
 111:   const leaseExpiresAt = new Date(Date.parse(now) + (deps.leaseDurationMs ?? 30_000)).toISOString();
 112:   const claim = await deps.markerStore.claim(record.documentRequestId, record.issuanceGeneration, now, leaseExpiresAt);
 113:   if (claim.outcome === "ALREADY_DELIVERED") return { kind: "ALREADY_DELIVERED" };
 114:   if (claim.outcome === "LEASE_ACTIVE") return { kind: "SKIPPED_LEASE_ACTIVE" };
 115:   if (claim.outcome === "PREVIOUSLY_UNCERTAIN") {
 116:     // Never call SES again - just make sure the human-facing alert actually got through.
 117:     await deps.notifyUncertainDelivery({
 118:       documentRequestId: record.documentRequestId,
 119:       issuanceGeneration: record.issuanceGeneration,
 120:       failureKind: claim.failureKind,
 121:       correlationId: deps.newCorrelationId(),
 122:     });
 123:     return { kind: "PREVIOUSLY_UNCERTAIN", failureKind: claim.failureKind };
 124:   }
 125: 
 126:   const { claimId } = claim;
 127:   const guestLink = `${deps.guestUploadBaseUrl}/${encodeURIComponent(record.token)}`;
 128:   try {
 129:     await deps.emailProvider.send({
 130:       to: request.recipientEmail,
 131:       templateId: "guest-credential-delivery-invite",
 132:       templateVersion: 1,
 133:       locale: "pt-BR",
 134:       renderContext: { deadlineLocal: request.deadline?.slice(0, 10), guestLink },
 135:       tags: { attemptId: `${record.documentRequestId}#${record.issuanceGeneration}`, intentId: record.documentRequestId, tenantId: record.tenantId, correlationId: deps.newCorrelationId() },
 136:     });
 137:   } catch (err) {
 138:     const failureKind: EmailSendFailureKind = err instanceof EmailSendError ? err.kind : "AMBIGUOUS";
 139:     if (failureKind === "CONCLUSIVE_RETRYABLE") {
 140:       // SES definitively refused the request BEFORE accepting it - never touched the boundary,
 141:       // safe for an immediate automatic retry.
 142:       await deps.markerStore.releaseClaim(record.documentRequestId, record.issuanceGeneration, claimId);
 143:       return { kind: "SEND_FAILED", error: err instanceof Error ? err.message : "SEND_FAILED" };
 144:     }
 145:     // AMBIGUOUS or CONCLUSIVE_TERMINAL - SES may already have accepted the message, or the
 146:     // error is too definitive to retry blindly. Never releaseClaim, never let Streams resend.
 147:     await transitionToUncertain(deps, record, claimId, failureKind, deps.now());
 148:     return { kind: "SEND_UNCERTAIN_NOT_RETRIED", failureKind };
 149:   }
 150: 
 151:   try {
 152:     await deps.markerStore.markDelivered(record.documentRequestId, record.issuanceGeneration, claimId, deps.now());
 153:     return { kind: "SENT" };
 154:   } catch {
 155:     // SES ALREADY accepted the send - a failure persisting DELIVERED is never grounds to
 156:     // release the claim (that would risk a real duplicate send on retry). Same uncertain path.
 157:     await transitionToUncertain(deps, record, claimId, "CONCLUSIVE_TERMINAL", deps.now());
 158:     return { kind: "SEND_UNCERTAIN_NOT_RETRIED", failureKind: "CONCLUSIVE_TERMINAL" };
 159:   }
 160: }
 161: 
 162: /** Marks the claim SEND_UNCERTAIN and alerts a human, in that order â€” if EITHER step throws,
 163:  * the exception propagates uncaught out of `deliverGuestCredential`, which the Lambda handler's
 164:  * own try/catch turns into a `batchItemFailures` entry (Streams retries). On retry, `claim()`
 165:  * finds the marker already `SEND_UNCERTAIN` (if the first step succeeded) and only the alert is
 166:  * retried â€” `emailProvider.send` is never called again for this record. */
 167: async function transitionToUncertain(
 168:   deps: GuestCredentialDeliveryDeps,
 169:   record: GuestCredentialDeliveryRecord,
 170:   claimId: string,
 171:   failureKind: string,
 172:   now: string,
 173: ): Promise<void> {
 174:   await deps.markerStore.markUncertain(record.documentRequestId, record.issuanceGeneration, claimId, failureKind, now);
 175:   await deps.notifyUncertainDelivery({
 176:     documentRequestId: record.documentRequestId,
 177:     issuanceGeneration: record.issuanceGeneration,
 178:     failureKind,
 179:     correlationId: deps.newCorrelationId(),
 180:   });
 181: }

 618: # --- GuestCredentialDelivery: guest_credential_delivery_table's OWN DynamoDB Streams (D-226
 619: # decision central item 5), closes the gap D-222/D-227 named â€” this worker did not exist until
 620: # D-228. Runs on the RESOURCE (non-guest) Lambda deployment unit: it only ever reads
 621: # DocumentRequest (tenant_facing_read_write_policy_json, same read access every other
 622: # document-archive consumer already has) and sends email via SES â€” it never touches the D-146
 623: # pepper, so it does not need to run on the guest Lambda's isolated deployment unit.
 624: #
 625: # Failure queue carries ONLY the failed batch's metadata (Lambda's on_failure destination
 626: # payload for a Streams source is {requestContext, DDBStreamBatchInfo} - no record content),
 627: # never the delivery record/token itself - D-226 decision central item 5's "reclassificada
 628: # como alerta" correction (D-226/D-227). A plain queue (no consumer, no DLQ of its own) is
 629: # enough - a human/runbook drains it, same posture reminder-dispatch-queue's own DLQ already
 630: # has before this design existed.
 631: resource "aws_sqs_queue" "guest_credential_delivery_failures" {
 632:   name                      = "${local.name_prefix}-guest-credential-delivery-failures"
 633:   message_retention_seconds = 14 * 24 * 60 * 60 # 14 days
 634:   sqs_managed_sse_enabled   = true
 635:   tags                      = { Project = local.project_name, Environment = var.environment }
 636: }

 640:   namespace           = "AWS/SQS"
 641:   metric_name         = "ApproximateNumberOfMessagesVisible"
 642:   dimensions          = { QueueName = aws_sqs_queue.guest_credential_delivery_failures.name }
 643:   statistic           = "Maximum"
 644:   period              = 300 # 5 minutes
 645:   evaluation_periods  = 1
 646:   threshold           = 0
 647:   comparison_operator = "GreaterThanThreshold"
 648:   alarm_description   = "GuestCredentialDeliveryFailures has metadata for at least one failed batch - investigate via runbook (queue never carries the raw token/record, only batch metadata)."
 649:   treat_missing_data  = "notBreaching"
 650:   alarm_actions       = [module.alert_topic.topic_arn]

 692: # D-233: SEC-R2-02 fix. Two changes from the original mapping:
 693: #  - filter_criteria now also matches on entityType - the marker's OWN Put/Update/Delete writes
 694: #    (D-233's new claim/lease mechanism) already generate stream INSERT/MODIFY events on this
 695: #    same table; without this filter they'd reach the handler, get classified "malformed" by
 696: #    isDeliveryRecord(), and inflate batchItemFailures/the failure queue with pure noise (a latent
 697: #    bug that predates D-233 too, since claim()'s original PutItem already wrote a marker row).
 698: #  - maximum_retry_attempts/maximum_record_age_in_seconds set to -1 (unlimited, bounded only by
 699: #    DynamoDB Streams' own native 24h retention) - a bounded retry count/age (the previous
 700: #    3/3600) could exhaust BEFORE the marker's 30s claim lease ever expires, leaving
 701: #    SKIPPED_LEASE_ACTIVE retries stuck with no path to the SEND_UNCERTAIN reconciliation
 702: #    deliver.ts relies on. The IteratorAge alarm below is the real backstop for a genuinely
 703: #    stuck/poison record - it fires in minutes, not after the 24h native retention discards it.
 704: resource "aws_lambda_event_source_mapping" "guest_credential_delivery_from_stream" {
 705:   event_source_arn               = module.guest_credential_delivery_table.stream_arn
 706:   function_name                  = module.guest_credential_delivery_handler.live_alias_arn
 707:   starting_position              = "LATEST"
 708:   batch_size                     = 5
 709:   function_response_types        = ["ReportBatchItemFailures"]
 710:   maximum_retry_attempts         = -1
 711:   maximum_record_age_in_seconds  = -1
 712:   bisect_batch_on_function_error = true
 713: 
 714:   filter_criteria {
 715:     filter {
 716:       pattern = jsonencode({
 717:         eventName = ["INSERT"]
 718:         dynamodb = {
 719:           NewImage = {
 720:             entityType = { S = ["GuestCredentialDelivery"] }
 721:           }
 722:         }
 723:       })
 724:     }
 725:   }
 726: 
 727:   destination_config {
 728:     on_failure {
 729:       destination_arn = aws_sqs_queue.guest_credential_delivery_failures.arn
 730:     }
 731:   }
 732: }
 733: 
 734: # D-233: real-time backstop replacing the "Errors alarm gives visibility" assumption that turned
 735: # out to be false - ReportBatchItemFailures means a retried record never surfaces as a Lambda
 736: # Errors metric, the invocation returns normally. IteratorAge is the correct signal: a record
 737: # stuck retrying (SKIPPED_LEASE_ACTIVE/SEND_FAILED looping) delays the shard's cursor immediately,
 738: # well before the stream's native 24h retention would silently discard it.
 739: resource "aws_cloudwatch_metric_alarm" "guest_credential_delivery_iterator_age" {
 740:   alarm_name          = "${local.name_prefix}-guest-credential-delivery-iterator-age"
 741:   namespace           = "AWS/Lambda"
 742:   metric_name         = "IteratorAge"
 743:   dimensions          = { FunctionName = module.guest_credential_delivery_handler.function_name }
 744:   statistic           = "Maximum"
 745:   period              = 300
 746:   evaluation_periods  = 1
 747:   threshold           = 300000 # 5 minutes in ms - far below the stream's 24h native retention.
 748:   comparison_operator = "GreaterThanThreshold"
 749:   alarm_description   = "guest-credential-delivery: stream cursor is falling behind - a record is being retried repeatedly (SKIPPED_LEASE_ACTIVE/SEND_FAILED) without converging to SENT/SEND_UNCERTAIN. Investigate before the stream's native 24h retention silently discards it."
 750:   treat_missing_data  = "notBreaching"
 751:   alarm_actions       = [module.alert_topic.topic_arn]
 752:   tags                = { Project = local.project_name, Environment = var.environment }
 753: }

 819: module "bff_handler" {
 820:   source = "./modules/lambda-function"
 821: 
 822:   function_name  = "${local.name_prefix}-bff-handler"
 823:   handler_name   = "bff-handler"
 824:   source_dir     = "${local.dist_dir}/bff-handler"
 825:   adot_layer_arn = var.adot_layer_arn
 826:   environment_variables = merge(local.common_env, {
 827:     BFF_SESSION_TABLE_NAME = module.bff_session_table.table_name
 828:     SESSION_TOKEN_PEPPER   = random_password.session_token_pepper.result
 829:     SESSION_KMS_KEY_ID     = module.bff_session_table.kms_key_id
 830:     COGNITO_USER_POOL_ID   = module.auth.user_pool_id
 831:     COGNITO_CLIENT_ID      = module.auth.user_pool_client_id
 832:     COGNITO_CLIENT_SECRET  = module.auth.user_pool_client_secret
 833:     COGNITO_DOMAIN         = "https://${module.auth.hosted_ui_domain}.auth.${var.aws_region}.amazoncognito.com"
 834:     BFF_REDIRECT_URI       = local.bff_redirect_uri
 835:     APP_ORIGIN             = var.app_origin
 836:     # Server-to-server calls stay on the real API's default execution endpoint (never
 837:     # through this same BFF's own API Gateway) - the JWT authorizer there is unaffected by
 838:     # any of this, exactly as designed (D-053: Full BFF is additive, resource routes keep
 839:     # accepting a direct Bearer token from any other caller too).
 840:     API_BASE_URL = module.api.api_endpoint
 841:     # Wave B2B-14 (Operational Evidence, D-115): real finding - `bff-handler.ts:32` has
 842:     # required this env var (module-level, eager) since Wave B2B-8/D-100 wired
 843:     # `handleAcceptInvitation` into the BFF, but this module was never updated to grant it -
 844:     # the BFF Lambda has been crashing with `Runtime.Unknown`/"GUEST_TOKEN_PEPPER env var is
 845:     # required" on EVERY cold start since that deploy, caught only now by the first real
 846:     # invocation against dev (no unit test mocks env vars at this layer, and no E2E test in
 847:     # this repo hits the real deployed backend - see multi-user-b2b-wave-b2b14-scope.md).
 848:     # Same pepper already granted to memberships_handler/guest_documents_handler/etc. below.
 849:     GUEST_TOKEN_PEPPER = random_password.guest_token_pepper.result
 850:   })
 851:   # Identity module reads/writes (User/IdentityMapping/DeviceSession) on the MAIN table -
 852:   # the SAME policy items_handler already uses, nothing new granted there. Session-table
 853:   # access is the separate, narrowly-scoped policy from bff_session_table (D-054: never
 854:   # merged into the general tenant_facing policy). gsi4_read_policy_json (Wave B2B-14/D-116):
 855:   # this is the Lambda whose real, live 500 ("DynamoDB access denied during
 856:   # OrganizationStore.queryGsi4") is the concrete evidence for this whole fix - bff-auth-service.ts
 857:   # calls OnboardingStateResolver.resolve() (queryGsi4) on every session/callback resolution.
 858:   policy_documents_json = [
 859:     module.table.tenant_facing_read_write_policy_json,
 860:     module.bff_session_table.bff_session_access_policy_json,
 861:     module.table.gsi4_read_policy_json,
 862:   ]
 863:   tags = { Project = local.project_name, Environment = var.environment }
 864: }

 892:   # P0.1 (auditoria externa 2026-09-11, D-283) - overrides the module's own default CSP only to
 893:   # add the quarantine bucket's virtual-hosted-style S3 origin to connect-src, the host
 894:   # AWS SDK v3's getSignedUrl already generates by default (confirmed in
 895:   # s3-upload-url-signer.ts) for the real browser->S3 presigned PUT. Every other directive is a
 896:   # literal copy of the module's own default (variables.tf's spa_content_security_policy) -
 897:   # never silently diverges if that default changes for an unrelated reason, since both live in
 898:   # the same PR review surface.
 899:   spa_content_security_policy = "default-src 'self'; connect-src 'self' https://${module.document_buckets.quarantine_bucket_name}.s3.${var.aws_region}.amazonaws.com; img-src 'self' data:; style-src 'self'; script-src 'self'; base-uri 'none'; object-src 'none'; form-action 'self'; frame-ancestors 'none'"
 900: }

1079:     sid       = "AllowEventBridgeRuleToSendMessage"
1080:     effect    = "Allow"
1081:     actions   = ["sqs:SendMessage"]
1082:     resources = [module.router_queue.queue_arn]
1083:     principals {
1084:       type        = "Service"
1085:       identifiers = ["events.amazonaws.com"]
1086:     }
1087:     condition {
1088:       test     = "ArnEquals"
1089:       variable = "aws:SourceArn"
1090:       values   = [aws_cloudwatch_event_rule.notification_intent_created.arn]

1221: # D-197 fatia 3/5 (D-10): first external-vendor secret in this repo. One JSON secret (access
1222: # token, app secret, phone number id, WABA id, verify token) - see
1223: # secrets-manager-whatsapp-config.ts's header comment for why not five separate secrets.
1224: # Terraform manages the secret's EXISTENCE and IAM access, never its real value - the
1225: # placeholder version below (all-empty-string JSON, matching D-229's own "safe to deploy before
1226: # real credentials exist" posture) is protected by `ignore_changes` so setting the real value
1227: # out-of-band (console/CLI, by Marcelo) is never clobbered by a later `terraform apply`.
1228: resource "aws_secretsmanager_secret" "whatsapp_cloud_api" {
1229:   name        = "${local.name_prefix}-whatsapp-cloud-api"

1402: # --- Trilha de auditoria de seguranÃ§a (MVP desta sessÃ£o) -----------------------------------
1403: # docs/architecture/reviews/security-audit-trail-design/codex-reconciliation-round2-final-design.md
1404: # Detecta os 2 achados reais abertos (SeguranÃ§a-Logging/OWASP A09, SRE-DetecÃ§Ã£o): negaÃ§Ã£o de
1405: # autorizaÃ§Ã£o e acesso a GSI3/GSI6 nÃ£o tinham trilha dedicada. Reusa o alert-topic real de M5.
1406: 
1407: # Real deploy failure (2026-08-28, PR #78, CD run 33210539869): brand-new profile_handler's log
1408: # group didn't exist yet when this module's PutMetricFilter tried to reference
1409: # "/aws/lambda/exptrk-dev-profile-handler" in the SAME apply that created the function -
1410: # ResourceNotFoundException, since a Lambda's log group is only auto-created by AWS on its FIRST
1411: # real invocation, which hadn't happened yet. Same bug class already fixed for malware_result/
1412: # reconciliation in document-observability (see that module's header comment) - fixed the same
1413: # way here instead of waiting for a real invocation to happen naturally.
1414: #
1415: # Logging-engineering review finding (Codex round 1, 2026-08-29): the lists below were also
1416: # genuinely INCOMPLETE, independent of the log-group-race bug - `documents_handler`/
1417: # `subjects_handler`/`imports_handler` all have real call sites of `auditAuthorizationDenied`
1418: # (document-handlers.ts/extraction-handlers.ts, subject-handlers.ts, import-handlers.ts) that
1419: # were never wired to a metric filter, and `document_purge_handler`/
1420: # `upload_slot_reconciliation_handler` both emit `security.global_index_access(_denied)` with a
1421: # `component` value the `GlobalIndexComponent` union in security-audit.ts explicitly names as a
1422: # real GSI6 consumer, also never wired. See `test/architecture/security-audit-observability-
1423: # coverage.test.ts` for the regression-proof cross-check (greps every real call site and asserts
1424: # its owning Lambda module appears in the two lists below) - the module-local Terraform test
1425: # (`infra/modules/security-audit-observability/tests/`) uses its own synthetic fixture and
1426: # cannot catch a root-wiring omission like this one.
1427: #
1428: # Of the 2 newly-added functions without a confirmed pre-existing log group (checked via `aws
1429: # logs describe-log-groups` against real `dev`, 2026-08-29): `subjects_handler`/
1430: # `imports_handler` had never been invoked in `dev` and need the same explicit-log-group
1431: # treatment as `profile_handler` above. `documents_handler` (real traffic already),
1432: # `document_purge_handler` (its EventBridge Scheduler already fired at least once), and
1433: # `upload_slot_reconciliation_handler` (already Terraform-managed by document-observability's
1434: # own `aws_cloudwatch_log_group.reconciliation`) all already have a real log group - adding a
1435: # managed resource for those would try to (re)create an existing one and fail with
1436: # ResourceAlreadyExistsException, same reasoning as the other 4 functions already noted above.
1437: resource "aws_cloudwatch_log_group" "subjects_handler" {
1438:   name              = "/aws/lambda/${module.subjects_handler.function_name}"
1439:   retention_in_days = 30
1440:   tags              = { Project = local.project_name, Environment = var.environment }
1441: }
1442: 
1443: resource "aws_cloudwatch_log_group" "imports_handler" {
