1566: # S3 "Object Created" in the quarantine bucket -> UploadFinalizerWorker. The quarantine
1567: # bucket's own aws_s3_bucket_notification (document-buckets module) forwards to EventBridge;
1568: # this is the rule/target/queue-policy triad that routes it onward (same shape as M4's
1569: # notification.intent-created.v1 rule below).
1570: resource "aws_cloudwatch_event_rule" "quarantine_object_created" {
1571:   name           = "${local.name_prefix}-quarantine-object-created"
1572:   event_bus_name = "default"
1573:   event_pattern = jsonencode({
1574:     source      = ["aws.s3"]
1575:     detail-type = ["Object Created"]
1576:     detail = {
1577:       bucket = { name = [module.document_buckets.quarantine_bucket_name] }
1578:     }
1579:   })
1580:   tags = { Project = local.project_name, Environment = var.environment }
1581: }
1582:

1584:   rule      = aws_cloudwatch_event_rule.quarantine_object_created.name
1585:   target_id = "upload-finalizer-queue"
1586:   arn       = module.upload_finalizer_queue.queue_arn
1587: }
1588: 
1589: data "aws_iam_policy_document" "eventbridge_to_upload_finalizer_queue" {
1590:   statement {
1591:     sid       = "AllowEventBridgeRuleToSendMessage"
1592:     effect    = "Allow"
1593:     actions   = ["sqs:SendMessage"]
1594:     resources = [module.upload_finalizer_queue.queue_arn]
1595:     principals {
1596:       type        = "Service"
1597:       identifiers = ["events.amazonaws.com"]
1598:     }
1599:     condition {
1600:       test     = "ArnEquals"
1601:       variable = "aws:SourceArn"
1602:       values   = [aws_cloudwatch_event_rule.quarantine_object_created.arn]
1603:     }
1604:   }
1605: }
1606:

1750: # --- UploadFinalizerWorker: S3 event (via queue) -> validate + invoke ParserSandbox ---------
1751: 
1752: # Real bug found via Camada 3 (2026-08-22): UploadFinalizerWorker only had READ access to the
1753: # quarantine bucket, but advanceAfterEvidence() (called identically by both workers) performs
1754: # the actual quarantine->clean promotion copy whichever worker's evidence completes the pair
1755: # LAST - which is not always MalwareResultWorker. A real upload where the finalizer's own
1756: # evidence-persist step landed after the malware scan result hit exactly this: the finalizer
1757: # won the PROMOTE race and got a real AccessDenied trying s3:PutObject on the clean bucket.
1758: # Both workers need IDENTICAL object-access permissions for this reason - kept as two
1759: # separately-named data sources (not a single shared one) so each function's IAM role
1760: # document stays self-explanatory in `aws iam` output, but the statements are deliberately
1761: # identical to malware_result_object_access below.
1762: data "aws_iam_policy_document" "upload_finalizer_object_access" {
1763:   statement {
1764:     sid       = "ReadDeleteQuarantineObjects"
1765:     effect    = "Allow"
1766:     actions   = ["s3:GetObject", "s3:GetObjectVersion", "s3:DeleteObjectVersion"]
1767:     resources = ["${module.document_buckets.quarantine_bucket_arn}/*"]
1768:   }
1769:   statement {
1770:     # Real bug found via Camada 3 (2026-08-22): advanceAfterEvidence() calls headObject() on
1771:     # the clean bucket right after copyObject() to VERIFY the promotion copy actually landed
1772:     # before confirming CLEAN - S3's HeadObject API requires the s3:GetObject IAM action (a
1773:     # well-known S3 quirk: there is no separate "HeadObject" IAM action), which was never
1774:     # granted here (only PutObject was). The copy itself succeeded every time; only the
1775:     # verification step 403'd, surfaced by the AWS SDK as an opaque "UnknownError" rather than
1776:     # a clean AccessDenied.
1777:     sid       = "ReadWriteCleanObjects"
1778:     effect    = "Allow"
1779:     actions   = ["s3:GetObject", "s3:GetObjectVersion", "s3:PutObject"]
1780:     resources = ["${module.document_buckets.clean_bucket_arn}/*"]
1781:   }
1782:   statement {
1783:     sid       = "DecryptQuarantineObjects"
1784:     effect    = "Allow"
1785:     actions   = ["kms:Decrypt"]
1786:     resources = [module.document_buckets.quarantine_kms_key_arn]
1787:   }
1788:   statement {
1789:     sid       = "EncryptCleanObjects"
1790:     effect    = "Allow"
1791:     actions   = ["kms:GenerateDataKey", "kms:Decrypt"]
1792:     resources = [module.document_buckets.clean_kms_key_arn]
1793:   }
1794: }
1795: 
1796: data "aws_iam_policy_document" "upload_finalizer_invoke_parser_sandbox" {
1797:   statement {
1798:     sid       = "InvokeParserSandbox"
1799:     effect    = "Allow"
1800:     actions   = ["lambda:InvokeFunction"]
1801:     resources = [module.parser_sandbox.function_arn]
1802:   }
1803: }
1804:

2066: # --- DocumentPurgeWorker: EventBridge Scheduler, every 6 hours (W3-06/D-061) ----------------
2067: # Fourth (of exactly four) role ever granted gsi6_read - see security-audit.ts's
2068: # GlobalIndexComponent "document-purge" and stack.tftest.hcl's updated GSI6 isolation
2069: # assertions. Minimal, purpose-scoped S3 permission: `s3:DeleteObjectVersion` only (never
2070: # `GetObject`/`DeleteObject`) on both document buckets - the worker only ever deletes a
2071: # specific, already-known object version (`cleanObject` or upload/malware evidence, never
2072: # `quarantineObject` - see `purge.ts`'s module doc), it never reads content.

2096:   # finalize transaction) round trips per invocation - 60s leaves the 15min purge lease
2097:   # (PURGE_LEASE_MS, purge.ts) at a 15x margin over the worst-case invocation duration.
2098:   timeout_seconds       = 60

2867: # --- M7 (extraÃ§Ã£o/OCR): TextractTaskHandler (items 3/4, D-035) -----------------------------
2868: # Real runtime for the ASL's only real Task state so far (`RunTextract`,
2869: # infra/state-machines/document-extraction.asl.json). Deliberately NOT gated by
2870: # `var.extraction_pipeline_enabled` for the same reason item 2's Lambda/queue/IAM aren't
2871: # gated either: the resources below are inert on their own. `START_OCR` can only ever run if
2872: # Step Functions invokes this function via `lambda:invoke.waitForTaskToken`, which requires
2873: # the `document_extraction` state machine to exist - it doesn't yet (`infra/modules/
2874: # extraction-workflow` is still deliberately uninstantiated, items 5-7's Lambdas don't exist).
2875: # Without that invocation, `startDocumentTextDetection` (the only paid call in this whole
2876: # handler) is never reached by any real event source, so leaving this infra always-on/
2877: # inspectable in `dev` carries the same "deployable but never triggered by real traffic" risk
2878: # profile item 2 already accepted for the identical reason.
2879: #
2880: # `module.textract_task_handler.live_alias_arn` is the ARN item 3's Terraform module
2881: # (extraction-workflow) needs once it's finally instantiated - exposed via
2882: # `local.textract_task_handler_function_arn` below so that wiring is a small diff, not a
2883: # redesign (same intent as `local.extraction_state_machine_arn` above).
2884: 
2885: # Dedicated CMK for the Step Functions task-token ciphertext (`TextractJob.taskTokenCiphertext`)
2886: # - same D-054 disciplina as `infra/modules/bff-session-table`'s refresh-token CMK: a live
2887: # Step Functions callback credential is exactly the class of "live credential, not just
2888: # storage-at-rest" value that decision reserved a dedicated CMK for, distinct from the
2889: # AWS-managed key `document-buckets` uses for document blobs (a much less sensitive value).
2890: # Reusing an existing CMK was considered (see NEXT_SESSION_PROMPT.md) and rejected: sharing a
2891: # key across two independently-deployable modules (bff/extraction) would couple their key
2892: # policies/rotation and blur the "only this module's Lambda role may ever use this key"
2893: # invariant D-054 established.
2894: resource "aws_kms_key" "task_token" {
2895:   description             = "CMK for TextractTaskHandler's Step Functions task-token ciphertext at rest (D-035)"
2896:   deletion_window_in_days = 30
2897:   enable_key_rotation     = true
2898:   tags                    = { Project = local.project_name, Environment = var.environment }
2899: }
2900: 
2901: resource "aws_kms_alias" "task_token" {
2902:   name          = "alias/${local.name_prefix}-task-token"
2903:   target_key_id = aws_kms_key.task_token.key_id
2904: }
2905: 
2906: # `EXTRACTION_TRANSIENT` S3 class (privacy-lgpd.md Â§4) - dedicated bucket, no versioning/
2907: # backup/replication (the artifact is disposable OCR text, never a document of record), 24h
2908: # lifecycle safety net matching `EXTRACTION_TRANSIENT_LIFECYCLE_HOURS` (retention.ts) exactly
2909: # (24h = 1 day, S3 lifecycle `expiration.days` has no hour granularity). Explicit deletion is
2910: # always `ExtractionValidationTaskHandler`'s job (item 7, implemented - run-extraction-validation.ts
2911: # calls artifacts.delete() at both terminal states) - this lifecycle rule only catches a run
2912: # that never reaches a terminal state.

3167: # `START_OCR` invocation permission for Step Functions - deliberately granted here even
3168: # though no state machine exists yet (same "deployable, not yet wired" posture as the rest of
3169: # this section): item 3's extraction-workflow module needs this permission to already exist
3170: # when it's finally instantiated, and granting invoke to a specific state machine ARN (rather
3171: # than "*") is safe to create ahead of time (`local.extraction_state_machine_arn`, already
3172: # defined above, is deterministic even before that state machine is created).
3173: resource "aws_lambda_permission" "textract_task_from_state_machine" {
3174:   statement_id = "AllowInvokeFromDocumentExtractionStateMachine"
3175:   action       = "lambda:InvokeFunction"
3176:   # `function_name` must be the UNQUALIFIED function ARN (`function_arn`, never
3177:   # `live_alias_arn`, which is already alias-qualified and redundant with `qualifier` below) -
3178:   # confirmed by reading the real deployed state directly (`terraform plan` against `dev`):
3179:   # AWS's own read-back of this resource's FunctionName always normalizes to the plain function
3180:   # ARN, so a declared `live_alias_arn` (or, tried first, the bare function NAME) never
3181:   # converges against it - both made this resource perpetually non-convergent, replaced on
3182:   # EVERY `terraform apply` on this repo regardless of what the apply was actually about,
3183:   # confirmed by 2 consecutive unrelated deploys (2026-09-12) each showing the identical
3184:   # 4-resource replacement. Found and fixed incidentally while verifying an unrelated deploy's
3185:   # plan output; re-verified against real `dev` state after the fix (0 replacements, not just
3186:   # `terraform validate` passing).
3187:   function_name = module.textract_task_handler.function_arn
3188:   qualifier     = module.textract_task_handler.live_alias_name
3189:   principal     = "states.amazonaws.com"
3190:   source_arn    = local.extraction_state_machine_arn
3191: }
3192: 
3193: locals {
3194:   # Item 3's extraction-workflow module wiring point - see the module's own `variables.tf`
3195:   # (parameterized by Lambda ARN variables, still not instantiated from infra/main.tf).
3196:   textract_task_handler_function_arn = module.textract_task_handler.live_alias_arn
3197: }

3353: # --- M7 (extraÃ§Ã£o/OCR): ExtractionValidationTaskHandler (item 7, D-035 Â§2/Â§3) --------------

3362: # writes the artifact's contents itself). No Textract/Bedrock/VPC/KMS/SNS/SQS/Step Functions
3363: # client, no AppConfig (the kill switches were already read/enforced by items 4/5/6 upstream).

3417: # --- M7 item 3: the real document-extraction Step Functions Standard state machine ---------
3418: # All four Lambdas the ASL references (items 4-7) now exist for real above - this instantiates
3419: # infra/modules/extraction-workflow/, which was deliberately left uncalled until now (an
3420: # aws_sfn_state_machine whose `definition` embeds a Lambda ARN fails terraform apply itself,
3421: # not just runtime, if any of those four functions doesn't exist yet).
3422: #
3423: # Gate discipline (same posture as items 2/4/5/6/7 above, D-035 Â§1.6): this resource, its
3424: # execution IAM role, and the four states.amazonaws.com invoke permissions (already granted on
3425: # each handler's own role, items 4-7) always exist - inspectable/deployable regardless of
3426: # var.extraction_pipeline_enabled. Nothing here is gated, because gating it would be
3427: # redundant: item 2 already gates the ONLY live entry point that can ever call
3428: # aws_sfn_state_machine:StartExecution on this state machine (the EventBridge rule connecting
3429: # the M6 clean-bucket event to ExtractionStarterWorker) - with that gate at `false` (default),
3430: # this state machine can exist, be inspected, and even be started manually for a scratch/test
3431: # execution, but never receives real traffic. A second gate at this layer would just duplicate
3432: # item 2's without adding any real protection.
3433: data "aws_iam_policy_document" "extraction_workflow_assume_role" {
3434:   statement {
3435:     sid     = "StatesAssumeRole"
3436:     effect  = "Allow"
3437:     actions = ["sts:AssumeRole"]
3438:     principals {
3439:       type        = "Service"
3440:       identifiers = ["states.amazonaws.com"]
3441:     }
3442:   }
3443: }
3444: 
3445: resource "aws_iam_role" "extraction_workflow_state_machine" {
3446:   name               = "${local.name_prefix}-extraction-workflow-role"
3447:   assume_role_policy = data.aws_iam_policy_document.extraction_workflow_assume_role.json
3448:   tags               = { Project = local.project_name, Environment = var.environment }
3449: }
3450: 
3451: # lambda:InvokeFunction on the exact four live-alias ARNs the ASL's Task states target -
3452: # never a wildcard. Each handler's own execution role already grants states.amazonaws.com
3453: # permission to invoke IT (aws_lambda_permission.*_from_state_machine, items 4-7 above,
3454: # scoped to this same state machine's deterministic ARN) - this is the other half of that
3455: # trust relationship, the state machine's own permission to call out to them.
3456: data "aws_iam_policy_document" "extraction_workflow_invoke_lambdas" {
3457:   statement {
3458:     sid    = "InvokeExtractionPipelineLambdas"
3459:     effect = "Allow"
3460:     actions = [
3461:       "lambda:InvokeFunction",
3462:     ]
3463:     resources = [
3464:       local.textract_task_handler_function_arn,
3465:       local.pdf_parser_task_handler_function_arn,
3466:       local.bedrock_extraction_task_handler_function_arn,
3467:       local.extraction_validation_task_handler_function_arn,
3468:     ]
3469:   }
3470: 
3471:   # CloudWatch Logs delivery for aws_sfn_state_machine's logging_configuration - AWS requires
3472:   # these exact actions on "*" (they operate on the account-level log delivery subsystem, not
3473:   # a specific log group ARN); documented by AWS as the minimum IAM for state machine logging.
3474:   statement {
3475:     sid    = "StateMachineLogDelivery"
3476:     effect = "Allow"
3477:     actions = [
3478:       "logs:CreateLogDelivery",
3479:       "logs:GetLogDelivery",
3480:       "logs:UpdateLogDelivery",
3481:       "logs:DeleteLogDelivery",
3482:       "logs:ListLogDeliveries",
3483:       "logs:PutResourcePolicy",
3484:       "logs:DescribeResourcePolicies",
3485:       "logs:DescribeLogGroups",
3486:     ]
3487:     resources = ["*"]
3488:   }
3489: 
3490:   # X-Ray - same AWSXRayDaemonWriteAccess-equivalent actions the lambda-function module
3491:   # attaches to every Lambda when tracing_active is true (main.tf's adot_layer_arn wiring),
3492:   # replicated here for the state machine's own X-Ray participation (tracing_configuration
3493:   # above). AWS's own X-Ray managed policy for Step Functions uses this same "*" resource.
3494:   statement {
3495:     sid    = "StateMachineXRayWrite"
3496:     effect = "Allow"
3497:     actions = [
3498:       "xray:PutTraceSegments",
3499:       "xray:PutTelemetryRecords",
3500:       "xray:GetSamplingRules",
3501:       "xray:GetSamplingTargets",
3502:     ]
3503:     resources = ["*"]
3504:   }
3505: }

3507: resource "aws_iam_role_policy" "extraction_workflow_state_machine" {
3508:   name   = "${local.name_prefix}-extraction-workflow-policy"
3509:   role   = aws_iam_role.extraction_workflow_state_machine.id
3510:   policy = data.aws_iam_policy_document.extraction_workflow_invoke_lambdas.json

3525: # =========================================================================================
3526: # W3-07 tenant purge orchestrator (D-124, implementing the design APPROVED as D-121).
3527: #
3528: # Before this block, `transitionTenantLifecycle()` (D-068) and `purgeTenant()` (W3-07) were both
3529: # real, working, fully-tested code with NO real caller anywhere in production - the single open
3530: # question D-083 left explicitly unanswered. This wires the trigger, the state machine, the two
3531: # Task Lambdas and the recurring sweeper that finally drive them.
3532: #
3533: # The state machine ARN is derived deterministically from its own name (same idiom as
3534: # `local.extraction_state_machine_arn` above) rather than read back from the module output: the
3535: # memberships handler needs the ARN in an env var, and the state machine needs the handler ARNs,
3536: # so reading the real attribute in both directions would be a Terraform dependency cycle.
3537: # `tenant-purge-workflow`'s own tftest asserts the name convention these two must agree on.
3538: # =========================================================================================
3539: 
3540: locals {
3541:   tenant_purge_state_machine_arn = "arn:aws:states:${var.aws_region}:${var.aws_account_id}:stateMachine:${local.name_prefix}-tenant-purge"
3542:   # D-127: every real execution ARN of this state machine, regardless of closureAttemptId suffix -
3543:   # StopExecution/DescribeExecution operate on EXECUTION arns, not the state machine arn itself
3544:   # (see `deriveExecutionArn` in sfn-tenant-purge-execution-starter.ts for the exact format this
3545:   # mirrors). Scoped to executions of THIS state machine only, never a wildcard across machines.
3546:   tenant_purge_execution_arn_pattern = "arn:aws:states:${var.aws_region}:${var.aws_account_id}:execution:${local.name_prefix}-tenant-purge:*"
3547: 
3548:   # Every Lambda that touches the purge pipeline needs the same four bucket names - the closed
3549:   # per-bucket prefix-root table lives in src/runtime/aws/composition/tenant-purge.ts, and these
3550:   # env vars are its only input.
3551:   tenant_purge_bucket_env = {
3552:     BFF_SESSION_TABLE_NAME           = module.bff_session_table.table_name
3553:     CLEAN_BUCKET_NAME                = module.document_buckets.clean_bucket_name
3554:     QUARANTINE_BUCKET_NAME           = module.document_buckets.quarantine_bucket_name
3555:     IMPORT_RAW_BUCKET_NAME           = module.import_bucket.bucket_name
3556:     EXTRACTION_TRANSIENT_BUCKET_NAME = aws_s3_bucket.extraction_transient.bucket
3557:   }
3558: }
3559: 
3560: # --- Purge worker: the S3 and session-table surface purgeTenant() actually needs ------------
3561: # D-121 Rodada 3 Fix 8's minimum IAM surface, verified against the real adapters rather than
3562: # copied: S3TenantPurgeAdapter calls ListObjectVersions/DeleteObjects/ListMultipartUploads/
3563: # AbortMultipartUpload, so it needs s3:ListBucketVersions + s3:ListBucketMultipartUploads on the
3564: # BUCKET and s3:DeleteObject/s3:DeleteObjectVersion/s3:AbortMultipartUpload on its OBJECTS.
3565: # Deliberately no s3:GetObject anywhere - a purge deletes, it never reads content (same discipline
3566: # as document_purge_object_access above).
3567: data "aws_iam_policy_document" "tenant_purge_worker_s3" {
3568:   statement {
3569:     sid     = "ListTenantOwnedBuckets"
3570:     effect  = "Allow"
3571:     actions = ["s3:ListBucket", "s3:ListBucketVersions", "s3:ListBucketMultipartUploads"]
3572:     resources = [
3573:       module.document_buckets.clean_bucket_arn,
3574:       module.document_buckets.quarantine_bucket_arn,
3575:       module.import_bucket.bucket_arn,
3576:       aws_s3_bucket.extraction_transient.arn,
3577:     ]
3578:   }
3579:   statement {
3580:     sid     = "DeleteTenantOwnedObjects"
3581:     effect  = "Allow"
3582:     actions = ["s3:DeleteObject", "s3:DeleteObjectVersion", "s3:AbortMultipartUpload"]
3583:     resources = [
3584:       "${module.document_buckets.clean_bucket_arn}/*",
3585:       "${module.document_buckets.quarantine_bucket_arn}/*",
3586:       "${module.import_bucket.bucket_arn}/*",
3587:       "${aws_s3_bucket.extraction_transient.arn}/*",
3588:     ]
3589:   }
3590: }
3591: 
3592: # The bff-session-table is a SEPARATE physical table with no GSI: the purge Scans it filtered by
3593: # the `tenantId` attribute and conditionally deletes matching Session rows. Deliberately narrow -
3594: # Scan/DeleteItem only, never Put/Update, and never the KMS key (the purge never decrypts a
3595: # refresh token, it only removes the row).
3596: data "aws_iam_policy_document" "tenant_purge_worker_session_table" {
3597:   statement {
3598:     sid       = "ScanAndDeleteTenantSessions"
3599:     effect    = "Allow"
3600:     actions   = ["dynamodb:Scan", "dynamodb:DeleteItem"]
3601:     resources = [module.bff_session_table.table_arn]
3602:   }
3603: }
3604: 
3605: # D-234 (E-018, Codex Rodada 3): the sweeper re-runs the SAME read passes as the worker above but
3606: # never deletes a session row - it was incorrectly inheriting tenant_purge_worker_session_table's
3607: # DeleteItem grant. This Scan-only variant closes that excess.
3608: data "aws_iam_policy_document" "tenant_purge_sweeper_session_table" {
3609:   statement {
3610:     sid       = "ScanTenantSessionsReadOnly"
3611:     effect    = "Allow"
3612:     actions   = ["dynamodb:Scan"]
3613:     resources = [module.bff_session_table.table_arn]
3614:   }
3615: }

3617: module "tenant_purge_worker_handler" {
3618:   source = "./modules/lambda-function"
3619:

3621:   handler_name   = "tenant-purge-worker-handler"
3622:   source_dir     = "${local.dist_dir}/tenant-purge-worker-handler"
3623:   adot_layer_arn = var.adot_layer_arn
3624:   # A purge attempt Scans the whole main table plus the session table and paginates every S3
3625:   # prefix. It is deliberately resumable (purgeTenant() returns a checkpoint and the ASL's Choice
3626:   # loop feeds it back), so this timeout does not need to cover a whole large tenant - it only
3627:   # needs to make real progress per attempt before the loop resumes it.
3628:   timeout_seconds       = 300
3629:   environment_variables = merge(local.common_env, local.tenant_purge_bucket_env)
3630:   # D-234 (E-018): dedicated Scan+GetItem+TransactWriteItems policy on the main table (base table
3631:   # only, no GSI), not the general tenant_facing_read_write_policy_json.
3632:   policy_documents_json = [
3633:     module.table.cross_tenant_scan_policy_json["tenant_purge_worker"],
3634:     data.aws_iam_policy_document.tenant_purge_worker_s3.json,
3635:     data.aws_iam_policy_document.tenant_purge_worker_session_table.json,
3636:   ]
3637:   tags = { Project = local.project_name, Environment = var.environment }
3638: }

3658:   statement {
3659:     sid       = "InvokeTenantPurgeTaskHandlers"
3660:     effect    = "Allow"
3661:     actions   = ["lambda:InvokeFunction"]
3662:     resources = [module.tenant_lifecycle_transition_handler.live_alias_arn, module.tenant_purge_worker_handler.live_alias_arn]
3663:   }
3664: 
3665:   # Required by the module's logging_configuration. Found during implementation by comparing
3666:   # against extraction_workflow_invoke_lambdas rather than assumed: without these, a state machine
3667:   # that declares a log destination fails at APPLY time, not at runtime. AWS requires these exact
3668:   # actions on "*" - they operate on the account-level log-delivery subsystem, not on a specific
3669:   # log group ARN.
3670:   statement {
3671:     sid    = "StateMachineLogDelivery"
3672:     effect = "Allow"
3673:     actions = [
3674:       "logs:CreateLogDelivery",
3675:       "logs:GetLogDelivery",
3676:       "logs:UpdateLogDelivery",
3677:       "logs:DeleteLogDelivery",
3678:       "logs:ListLogDeliveries",
3679:       "logs:PutResourcePolicy",
3680:       "logs:DescribeResourcePolicies",
3681:       "logs:DescribeLogGroups",
3682:     ]
3683:     resources = ["*"]
3684:   }
3685: 
3686:   # Required by the module's tracing_configuration - same actions AWS's own X-Ray managed policy
3687:   # for Step Functions uses, and the same set extraction_workflow's role already carries.
3688:   statement {
3689:     sid    = "StateMachineXRayWrite"
3690:     effect = "Allow"
3691:     actions = [
3692:       "xray:PutTraceSegments",
3693:       "xray:PutTelemetryRecords",
3694:       "xray:GetSamplingRules",
3695:       "xray:GetSamplingTargets",
3696:     ]
3697:     resources = ["*"]
3698:   }
3699: }

3767: # --- The sweeper: EventBridge Scheduler, daily ---------------------------------------------
3768: # `rate`, not `cron` (same choice reminder-schedule makes for its non-time-of-day schedules): the
3769: # sweeper only needs a regular cadence, never a specific wall-clock hour. Daily is proposed by the
3770: # approved design and is explicitly NOT load-bearing - the repair half is bounded by the 1-hour
3771: # staleness filter, not by how often this runs.
3772: module "tenant_purge_sweeper_handler" {
3773:   source = "./modules/lambda-function"
3774:

3776:   handler_name    = "tenant-purge-sweeper-handler"
3777:   source_dir      = "${local.dist_dir}/tenant-purge-sweeper-handler"
3778:   adot_layer_arn  = var.adot_layer_arn
3779:   timeout_seconds = 300
3780:   environment_variables = merge(local.common_env, local.tenant_purge_bucket_env, {
3781:     TENANT_PURGE_STATE_MACHINE_ARN = local.tenant_purge_state_machine_arn
3782:   })
3783:   # The sweeper re-runs the SAME verifyTenant*Empty() passes the purge worker does, so it needs the
3784:   # same read surface - but it never deletes anything. D-234 (E-018, Codex Rodada 3): it now gets
3785:   # its OWN read-only DynamoDB policies (main table Scan+GetItem via cross_tenant_scan_policy_json
3786:   # ["tenant_purge_sweeper"]; session table Scan-only via tenant_purge_sweeper_session_table) rather
3787:   # than inheriting the worker's read/write grants (which included DeleteItem on both tables - the
3788:   # exact class of excess this finding closes). S3 policy is still shared with the worker
3789:   # (tenant_purge_worker_s3, which includes DeleteObject) - Codex Rodada 3 flagged this as a real
3790:   # but non-blocking follow-up for E-018 (scope is DynamoDB least-privilege); tracked as a nominal
3791:   # cleanup, not a new numbered finding.
3792:   policy_documents_json = [
3793:     module.table.cross_tenant_scan_policy_json["tenant_purge_sweeper"],
3794:     data.aws_iam_policy_document.tenant_purge_worker_s3.json,
3795:     data.aws_iam_policy_document.tenant_purge_sweeper_session_table.json,
3796:     data.aws_iam_policy_document.tenant_purge_start_execution.json,
3797:     # D-127: states:DescribeExecution for the HELD_FOR_RECOVERY reconciliation branch.
3798:     data.aws_iam_policy_document.tenant_purge_describe_execution.json,
3799:   ]
3800:   tags = { Project = local.project_name, Environment = var.environment }
3801: }

   5:  * This file exists specifically so m5-observability-design.md Â§5's "partial batch failure
   6:  * preserva isolamento" test (test/unit/dispatch-outbox-relay-handler.test.ts) can inject a
   7:  * fake RelayDeps/logger sink without touching AWS. */
   8: import type { DynamoDBRecord } from "aws-lambda";
   9: import { unmarshall } from "@aws-sdk/util-dynamodb";
  10: import { relayStreamRecord, type RelayDeps } from "../../../workers/dispatch-outbox-relay/relay.js";
  11: import type { OutboxRecord } from "../../../shared/outbox/outbox.js";
  12: import { defaultSchemaRegistry } from "../../../shared/contracts/schema-validator.js";
  13: import { runWithContext } from "../../../shared/observability/context.js";
  14: import type { SecureLogger } from "../../../shared/observability/logger.js";
  15: 
  16: // P2.1 (external audit 2026-09-11): this boundary used to go straight from `unmarshall` to a
  17: // bare TypeScript cast, with no runtime check that the Streams image actually has the shape
  18: // `OutboxRecord` claims - same class of gap `reminder-dispatch-handler.ts`'s
  19: // `DISPATCH_COMMAND_SCHEMA_ID` validation already closes for its own SQS boundary, mirrored
  20: // here rather than invented fresh.
  21: const OUTBOX_RECORD_SCHEMA_ID = "https://expiration-tracker/schemas/events/outbox-record.v1.json";
  22: 
  23: export async function processStreamRecords(
  24:   deps: Omit<RelayDeps, "leaseOwner">,
  25:   logger: SecureLogger,
  26:   records: DynamoDBRecord[],
  27: ): Promise<{ itemIdentifier: string }[]> {
  28:   const batchItemFailures: { itemIdentifier: string }[] = [];
  29: 
  30:   for (const record of records) {
  31:     if (record.eventName !== "INSERT" && record.eventName !== "MODIFY") continue;
  32:     const image = record.dynamodb?.NewImage;
  33:     if (!image) continue;
  34: 
  35:     try {
  36:       const raw: unknown = unmarshall(image as Record<string, never>);
  37:       // Streams captures EVERY write to this table, not just OutboxEvent rows - this filter
  38:       // stays BEFORE schema validation so every other entity type flowing through the same
  39:       // stream is skipped silently, same as always, never validated against (and never
  40:       // spamming a schema-invalid error for) a schema it was never meant to match.
  41:       if ((raw as { entityType?: unknown }).entityType !== "OutboxEvent") continue;
  42: 
  43:       const { valid, errors } = defaultSchemaRegistry.validate(OUTBOX_RECORD_SCHEMA_ID, raw);
  44:       if (!valid) {
  45:         logger.error("dispatch-outbox-relay schema-invalid OutboxEvent image", { eventID: record.eventID, errors });
  46:         batchItemFailures.push({ itemIdentifier: record.eventID ?? "" });
  47:         continue;
  48:       }
  49:       const item = raw as OutboxRecord;
  50: 
  51:       // m5-observability-design.md #2: DynamoDB Streams fallback is the record's own
  52:       // SequenceNumber, not eventId (that fallback is the sweeper's, via
  53:       // outboxRecordCorrelationId - a different source per the design's table).
  54:       const correlationId = item.correlationId ?? record.dynamodb?.SequenceNumber ?? record.eventID ?? "unknown";
  55:       await runWithContext({ correlationId }, async () => {
  56:         // try/catch stays INSIDE runWithContext so a failure log still carries this
  57:         // record's correlationId - a catch wrapping runWithContext itself would run after
  58:         // AsyncLocalStorage.run() already restored the outer (empty) context. This is also
  59:         // what proves partial batch failure preserves isolation (Â§5): record N+1 gets its
  60:         // own runWithContext call regardless of whether record N's callback threw.
  61:         try {
  62:           const outcome = await relayStreamRecord({ ...deps, leaseOwner: `relay-${record.eventID}` }, item);
  63:           logger.info("dispatch-outbox-relay outcome", { eventId: item.eventId, outcome: outcome.kind });
  64:           if (outcome.kind === "FAILED") {
  65:             batchItemFailures.push({ itemIdentifier: record.eventID ?? "" });
  66:           }
  67:         } catch (err) {
  68:           logger.error("dispatch-outbox-relay failed", { eventID: record.eventID, error: err instanceof Error ? err.message : String(err) });
  69:           batchItemFailures.push({ itemIdentifier: record.eventID ?? "" });
  70:         }
  71:       });
  72:     } catch (err) {
  73:       // Genuinely unparseable Streams image - no correlationId available at all.
  74:       logger.error("dispatch-outbox-relay failed to parse Streams image", { eventID: record.eventID, error: err instanceof Error ? err.message : String(err) });
  75:       batchItemFailures.push({ itemIdentifier: record.eventID ?? "" });
  76:     }
  77:   }
  78: 
  79:   return batchItemFailures;
  80: }
FILE: src/workers/dispatch-outbox-relay/relay.ts
  35: >;
  36: 
  37: export interface RelayDeps {
  38:   store: OutboxRelayStore;
  39:   senders: DestinationSenders;
  40:   now: () => string;
  41:   leaseOwner: string;
  42:   /** How long a lease is held while attempting SendMessage - default 30s, comfortably
  43:    * longer than one SQS SendMessage call, short enough that a crashed relay invocation's
  44:    * lease is quickly reclaimable by the next relay/sweeper attempt. */
  45:   leaseDurationMs?: number;
  46: }
  47: 
  48: export type PublishOutcome =
  49:   | { kind: "PUBLISHED" }
  50:   | { kind: "SKIPPED_WRONG_DESTINATION" }
  51:   | { kind: "SKIPPED_ALREADY_PUBLISHED" }
  52:   | { kind: "SKIPPED_LEASE_HELD" }
  53:   | { kind: "FAILED"; error: unknown };
  54: 
  55: /** Attempts to publish exactly one outbox record. Never throws for expected outcomes
  56:  * (wrong destination / already published / lease contention) - only for genuinely
  57:  * unexpected failures during acquire/send/mark, surfaced as `FAILED` so the caller (relay
  58:  * Lambda: partial batch failure; sweeper: per-item log+continue) decides retry policy. */
  59: export async function publishOne(deps: RelayDeps, record: OutboxRecord): Promise<PublishOutcome> {
  60:   // Exclusivity (docs/architecture/m3.5-runtime-design.md Â§"DecisÃ£o central"): this relay/
  61:   // sweeper NEVER touches a record it doesn't own the destination for - OutboxPublisher
  62:   // (generic, EventBridge) owns everything else, including records with no `destination`.
  63:   const send = record.destination ? deps.senders[record.destination] : undefined;
  64:   if (!send) {
  65:     return { kind: "SKIPPED_WRONG_DESTINATION" };
  66:   }
  67:   if (record.status === "PUBLISHED") {
  68:     return { kind: "SKIPPED_ALREADY_PUBLISHED" };
  69:   }
  70: 
  71:   // m5-observability-design.md #2: one runWithContext per record - correlationId read from
  72:   // the persisted OutboxRecord (the business operation that originally created it), never
  73:   // from this invocation's own ambient context.
  74:   return runWithContext({ correlationId: outboxRecordCorrelationId(record) }, () => publishAcquiredOrPending(deps, record, send));
  75: }
  76: 
  77: async function publishAcquiredOrPending(
  78:   deps: RelayDeps,
  79:   record: OutboxRecord,
  80:   send: (payload: Record<string, unknown>, correlationId: string, tenantId: string, eventType: string) => Promise<void>,
  81: ): Promise<PublishOutcome> {
  82:   const leaseDurationMs = deps.leaseDurationMs ?? 30_000;
  83:   const now = deps.now();
  84:   const leaseExpiresAt = new Date(Date.parse(now) + leaseDurationMs).toISOString();
  85: 
  86:   const acquired = await deps.store.tryAcquireLease({ PK: record.PK, SK: record.SK }, deps.leaseOwner, leaseExpiresAt, now);
  87:   if (!acquired) {
  88:     return { kind: "SKIPPED_LEASE_HELD" };
  89:   }
  90: 
  91:   try {
  92:     await send(record.payload, outboxRecordCorrelationId(record), record.tenantId, record.eventType);
  93:   } catch (error) {
  94:     // Failure here is expected and recoverable: the lease expires, the sweeper (or a later
  95:     // Stream retry) will retry. A duplicate SendMessage after a retry is absorbed by the
  96:     // downstream worker's own idempotency - never treated as a reason to skip retrying.
  97:     return { kind: "FAILED", error };
  98:   }
  99: 
 100:   // Transition PENDING -> PUBLISHED only AFTER SendMessage confirmed (m3.5-runtime-design.md:
 101:   // "falha entre (2) e (3) Ã© aceitÃ¡vel - duplicata Ã© absorvida pela idempotÃªncia do
 102:   // dispatch"). Failure here also just means the sweeper resends a message that was
 103:   // already delivered once - still recoverable, not a correctness issue.
 104:   await deps.store.markPublished({ PK: record.PK, SK: record.SK });
 105:   return { kind: "PUBLISHED" };
 106: }
 107: 
 108: /** DispatchOutboxRelay / NotificationEmailOutboxRelay entrypoint logic: one call per
 109:  * DynamoDB Streams record (NEW_IMAGE). Which record types actually flow through a given
 110:  * Lambda's Streams filter, and thus which `senders` it needs, is a wiring concern, not this
 111:  * function's - it stays destination-agnostic. */
 112: export async function relayStreamRecord(deps: RelayDeps, record: OutboxRecord): Promise<PublishOutcome> {
 113:   return publishOne(deps, record);
 114: }
 115: 
 116: export interface SweeperDeps extends RelayDeps {
 117:   /** Minimum age (ms) before a PENDING record is swept - covers relay/Stream failure, not
 118:    * normal in-flight latency (m3.5-runtime-design.md: "occurredAt mais antigo que um
 119:    * limiar (2 min)"). */
 120:   minAgeMs?: number;
 121:   pageSize?: number;
 122:   /** Which destinations to sweep this run - the sweeper queries GSI6 once per destination
 123:    * (same global partition, `RECON#OUTBOX#PENDING`, filtered by `destination` server-side by
 124:    * the store). Defaults to every destination this deps' `senders` map recognizes. */
 125:   destinations?: OutboxDestination[];

  30: const logger = new SecureLogger({ baseContext: { service: "guest-credential-delivery" } });
  31: 
  32: function isDeliveryRecord(value: unknown): value is GuestCredentialDeliveryRecord {
  33:   const v = value as Partial<GuestCredentialDeliveryRecord> | undefined;
  34:   return typeof v?.tenantId === "string" && typeof v?.subjectId === "string" && typeof v?.documentRequestId === "string" && typeof v?.issuanceGeneration === "number" && typeof v?.token === "string";
  35: }
  36: 
  37: export async function handler(event: DynamoDBStreamEvent): Promise<DynamoDBBatchResponse> {
  38:   const batchItemFailures: { itemIdentifier: string }[] = [];
  39: 
  40:   for (const streamRecord of event.Records) {
  41:     if (streamRecord.eventName !== "INSERT") continue;
  42:     const image = streamRecord.dynamodb?.NewImage;
  43:     if (!image) continue;
  44: 
  45:     await runWithContext({ correlationId: streamRecord.dynamodb?.SequenceNumber ?? streamRecord.eventID ?? "unknown" }, async () => {
  46:       try {
  47:         const record = unmarshall(image as Record<string, never>) as unknown;
  48:         if (!isDeliveryRecord(record) || record.entityType !== "GuestCredentialDelivery") {
  49:           logger.error("guest-credential-delivery malformed Streams image", { eventID: streamRecord.eventID });
  50:           batchItemFailures.push({ itemIdentifier: streamRecord.eventID ?? "" });
  51:           return;
  52:         }
  53:         await runWithContext({ correlationId: streamRecord.eventID ?? "unknown", tenantId: record.tenantId }, async () => {
  54:           const outcome = await deliverGuestCredential(deps, record);
  55:           logger.info("guest-credential-delivery outcome", { documentRequestId: record.documentRequestId, outcome: outcome.kind });
  56:           // D-233: SKIPPED_LEASE_ACTIVE must be retried like SEND_FAILED, never treated as a
  57:           // completed/successful invocation - it's how a redelivery keeps happening until the
  58:           // claim's lease expires and reconciles (see deliver.ts's header comment).
  59:           if (outcome.kind === "SEND_FAILED" || outcome.kind === "SKIPPED_LEASE_ACTIVE") {
  60:             batchItemFailures.push({ itemIdentifier: streamRecord.eventID ?? "" });
  61:           }
  62:         });
  63:       } catch (err) {
  64:         logger.error("guest-credential-delivery failed", { eventID: streamRecord.eventID, error: err instanceof Error ? err.message : String(err) });
  65:         batchItemFailures.push({ itemIdentifier: streamRecord.eventID ?? "" });
  66:       }
  67:     });
  68:   }
  69: 
  70:   return { batchItemFailures };
  71: }
FILE: src/workers/guest-credential-delivery/deliver.ts
   1: /**
   2:  * GuestCredentialDeliveryWorker â€” D-228 (closes D-222 Achado 1 for real), revised by D-233
   3:  * (fixes SEC-R2-02/full-audit-round2, both SeguranÃ§a and Arquitetura axes converged on the
   4:  * same finding independently): the original `deliver.ts` claimed the delivery marker BEFORE
   5:  * calling `emailProvider.send()` with no way back â€” a transient send failure (SES throttled,
   6:  * momentarily unavailable) permanently and silently lost the guest's only credential link,
   7:  * because a Streams retry found the marker already claimed and returned `ALREADY_DELIVERED`
   8:  * instead of resending. Full mechanism/decision record: `docs/architecture/decisions-log.md`
   9:  * D-233; Claude<->Codex convergence: `docs/engineering/reviews/d-233-*` (8 rounds, both â‰¥9.0).
  10:  *
  11:  * Consumes ONE `GuestCredentialDeliveryRecord` (already unmarshalled from the dedicated table's
  12:  * own DynamoDB Streams NEW_IMAGE, INSERT only â€” the record is created exactly once per issuance,
  13:  * never updated) and, if a recipient is known, emails the guest link that actually authenticates
  14:  * via `resolveCredential()`.
  15:  *
  16:  * Deliberately re-reads the authoritative `DocumentRequest` (main table) rather than trusting
  17:  * anything beyond the record's own key fields â€” same discipline
  18:  * `DocumentRequestCredentialIssuanceService.handle` already documents for the producer side.
  19:  * Two independent staleness/eligibility guards before ever sending:
  20:  *  - `request.issuanceGeneration !== record.issuanceGeneration`: a newer reissuance
  21:  *    (`rejectVersion()`) has already superseded this delivery â€” safe no-op.
  22:  *  - `!request.recipientEmail`: a genuine data gap (D-228 pendency â€” series-materialized
  23:  *    requests carry no recipient contact yet), terminal and NOT retried; retrying can never
  24:  *    make an absent field appear.
  25:  *
  26:  * Idempotency under Streams' at-least-once redelivery (G-V3 requirement), REVISED mechanism
  27:  * (D-233): a lease-based claim, proportional variant of the same PRINCIPLE
  28:  * `NotificationAttempt`/`email-delivery-workflow.ts` already established for this exact problem
  29:  * class (never `NotificationAttempt`'s full OCC/version machinery â€” a guest link is a static,
  30:  * harmlessly re-sendable value, unlike a customer-facing paid send).
  31:  *  - `markerStore.claim()` returns `CLAIMED` (safe to send), `LEASE_ACTIVE` (another invocation
  32:  *    is sending right now or died very recently â€” retryable, never a silent skip),
  33:  *    `ALREADY_DELIVERED` (terminal, confirmed sent), or `PREVIOUSLY_UNCERTAIN` (a prior attempt
  34:  *    could not conclusively rule out having sent â€” terminal for automated resend, alerted).
  35:  *  - `EmailProviderAdapter`'s existing `EmailSendFailureKind` taxonomy
  36:  *    (`CONCLUSIVE_RETRYABLE`/`CONCLUSIVE_TERMINAL`/`AMBIGUOUS`, `email-provider.ts`) decides the
  37:  *    outcome: ONLY `CONCLUSIVE_RETRYABLE` (SES definitively rejected the request before
  38:  *    accepting it) releases the claim for an immediate, safe automatic retry. Every other
  39:  *    outcome (`AMBIGUOUS`, `CONCLUSIVE_TERMINAL`, or the send succeeding but `markDelivered`
  40:  *    itself failing to persist) transitions to `SEND_UNCERTAIN` â€” SES may have already accepted
  41:  *    the message, so a duplicate send would be a real, avoidable failure â€” paired ALWAYS with
  42:  *    `notifyUncertainDelivery` (an alert into the existing `guest-credential-delivery-failures`
  43:  *    queue/alarm, never silent, never carrying the raw token).
  44:  *  - A claim whose lease expires without ANY of the above transitions (process died at an
  45:  *    indeterminate point, including possibly after SES already accepted) is reconciled by
  46:  *    `claim()` ITSELF into `SEND_UNCERTAIN` on the next invocation â€” never resent automatically.
  47:  *
  48:  * The raw token (`record.token`) is embedded in the email body â€” its ONLY appearance outside
  49:  * the dedicated delivery table â€” and never logged, never written to any other row (the alert
  50:  * payload for SEND_UNCERTAIN carries only key/metadata fields, never the token).
  51:  */
  52: import { EmailSendError, type EmailSendFailureKind, type EmailProviderAdapter } from "../../modules/notification/ports/email-provider.js";
  53: import type { GuestCredentialDeliveryMarkerStore } from "../../modules/document-archive/ports/guest-credential-delivery-marker-store.js";
  54: import { documentRequestKey, type DocumentRequest } from "../../modules/document-archive/domain/document-request.js";
  55: import type { GuestCredentialDeliveryRecord } from "../../modules/document-archive/domain/guest-credential-delivery.js";
  56: import { authorizedTenantIdFromPersistedEntity } from "../../modules/identity/domain/authorization.js";
  57:

  59:   get<T extends { PK: string; SK: string } = DocumentRequest & { PK: string; SK: string }>(key: { PK: string; SK: string }): Promise<T | undefined>;
  60: }

  64:   issuanceGeneration: number;
  65:   failureKind: string;
  66:   correlationId: string;
  67: }
  68: 
  69: export interface GuestCredentialDeliveryDeps {
  70:   store: GuestCredentialDeliveryStore;
  71:   markerStore: GuestCredentialDeliveryMarkerStore;
  72:   emailProvider: EmailProviderAdapter;
  73:   /** Sends an operator-facing alert (metadata only, never the token) when a delivery could not
  74:    * be conclusively resolved as sent or not-sent â€” real implementation is an SQS SendMessage
  75:    * into the already-existing `guest-credential-delivery-failures` queue (composition root),
  76:    * kept as an injected dependency so this worker stays AWS-SDK-free (AGENTS.md Â§7). */
  77:   notifyUncertainDelivery: (alert: UncertainDeliveryAlert) => Promise<void>;
  78:   /** Base URL of G02's real route (`docs/frontend/prototype-screen-specs/
  79:    * G02-solicitacao-documento-convidado.md`'s own route contract:
  80:    * `/document-archive/guest/document-requests/:token`, a PATH segment â€” never `?token=`, the
  81:    * placeholder shape this deliberately superseded once the frontend route existed to conform
  82:    * to, Block 6/D-2xx). No trailing slash â€” `deliverGuestCredential` appends `/${token}`. */
  83:   guestUploadBaseUrl: string;
  84:   now: () => string;

  86:   /** How long a claim is leased before an unresolved invocation is treated as died-mid-flight

  88:    * resolves synchronously via releaseClaim/markDelivered/markUncertain; the lease only covers
  89:    * the residual case of a process dying with no observable outcome at all). */
  90:   leaseDurationMs?: number;
  91: }
  92: 
  93: export type GuestCredentialDeliveryOutcome =
  94:   | { kind: "SENT" }
  95:   | { kind: "ALREADY_DELIVERED" }
  96:   | { kind: "SKIPPED_REQUEST_NOT_FOUND" }
  97:   | { kind: "SKIPPED_STALE_GENERATION" }
  98:   | { kind: "SKIPPED_NO_RECIPIENT_EMAIL" }
  99:   | { kind: "SKIPPED_LEASE_ACTIVE" }
 100:   | { kind: "SEND_FAILED"; error: string }
 101:   | { kind: "SEND_UNCERTAIN_NOT_RETRIED"; failureKind: string }
 102:   | { kind: "PREVIOUSLY_UNCERTAIN"; failureKind: string };
 103: 
 104: export async function deliverGuestCredential(deps: GuestCredentialDeliveryDeps, record: GuestCredentialDeliveryRecord): Promise<GuestCredentialDeliveryOutcome> {
 105:   const request = await deps.store.get<DocumentRequest>(documentRequestKey(authorizedTenantIdFromPersistedEntity(record), record.subjectId, record.documentRequestId));
 106:   if (!request) return { kind: "SKIPPED_REQUEST_NOT_FOUND" };
 107:   if (request.issuanceGeneration !== record.issuanceGeneration) return { kind: "SKIPPED_STALE_GENERATION" };
 108:   if (!request.recipientEmail) return { kind: "SKIPPED_NO_RECIPIENT_EMAIL" };
 109: 
 110:   const now = deps.now();
 111:   const leaseExpiresAt = new Date(Date.parse(now) + (deps.leaseDurationMs ?? 30_000)).toISOString();
 112:   const claim = await deps.markerStore.claim(record.documentRequestId, record.issuanceGeneration, now, leaseExpiresAt);
 113:   if (claim.outcome === "ALREADY_DELIVERED") return { kind: "ALREADY_DELIVERED" };
 114:   if (claim.outcome === "LEASE_ACTIVE") return { kind: "SKIPPED_LEASE_ACTIVE" };
 115:   if (claim.outcome === "PREVIOUSLY_UNCERTAIN") {
 116:     // Never call SES again - just make sure the human-facing alert actually got through.
 117:     await deps.notifyUncertainDelivery({
 118:       documentRequestId: record.documentRequestId,
 119:       issuanceGeneration: record.issuanceGeneration,
 120:       failureKind: claim.failureKind,
 121:       correlationId: deps.newCorrelationId(),
 122:     });
 123:     return { kind: "PREVIOUSLY_UNCERTAIN", failureKind: claim.failureKind };
 124:   }

/**
 * SecureLogger - implementation-blueprint.md #14.1: "Todos os handlers usam SecureLogger.
 * Chamadas diretas a console.* falham no lint" (enforced by .eslintrc.cjs no-console rule,
 * scoped-out only for this file). Structured JSON to stdout (CloudWatch Logs picks it up),
 * every field passed through the central Redactor first.
 *
 * tenantId may appear in structured logs for investigation (per #14.1) but must never become
 * an EMF metric dimension. NOTE (full-audit round1/qualidade, 2026-08-19): no
 * shared/observability/metrics.ts exists yet - this constraint is aspirational until that
 * module is built (tracked as a real gap under Observability & Operability, not this logger's
 * job to enforce). Do not treat this comment as evidence metrics.ts exists.
 */
import { getContext } from "./context.js";
import { defaultRedactor, Redactor } from "./redactor.js";
import { parseXrayTraceHeader } from "./xray-trace-header.js";

export type LogLevel = "debug" | "info" | "warn" | "error";

export interface LogContext {
  correlationId?: string;
  tenantId?: string;
  requestId?: string;
  [key: string]: unknown;
}

export interface SecureLoggerOptions {
  redactor?: Redactor;
  /** Base context merged into every log line (e.g. service name, function name). */
  baseContext?: LogContext;
  /** Injectable sink for tests; defaults to console. */
  sink?: (level: LogLevel, line: string) => void;
  now?: () => string;
}

// Merges left-to-right, but a source's `undefined` value for a key never overwrites a value
// already set by an earlier (less trusted) source - only a genuinely present value can win.
function mergeDefined(...sources: object[]): Record<string, unknown> {
  const result: Record<string, unknown> = {};
  for (const source of sources) {
    for (const [key, value] of Object.entries(source)) {
      if (value !== undefined) {
        result[key] = value;
      }
    }
  }
  return result;
}

const defaultSink = (level: LogLevel, line: string): void => {
  const fn = level === "error" ? console.error : level === "warn" ? console.warn : console.log;
  fn(line);
};

export class SecureLogger {
  private readonly redactor: Redactor;
  private readonly baseContext: LogContext;
  private readonly sink: (level: LogLevel, line: string) => void;
  private readonly now: () => string;

  constructor(options: SecureLoggerOptions = {}) {
    this.redactor = options.redactor ?? defaultRedactor;
    this.baseContext = options.baseContext ?? {};
    this.sink = options.sink ?? defaultSink;
    this.now = options.now ?? (() => new Date().toISOString());
  }

  /** Returns a new logger with additional persistent context (e.g. bind correlationId once per invocation). */
  child(context: LogContext): SecureLogger {
    return new SecureLogger({
      redactor: this.redactor,
      baseContext: { ...this.baseContext, ...context },
      sink: this.sink,
      now: this.now,
    });
  }

  debug(event: string, context: LogContext = {}): void {
    this.write("debug", event, context);
  }

  info(event: string, context: LogContext = {}): void {
    this.write("info", event, context);
  }

  warn(event: string, context: LogContext = {}): void {
    this.write("warn", event, context);
  }

  error(event: string, context: LogContext = {}): void {
    this.write("error", event, context);
  }

  private write(level: LogLevel, event: string, context: LogContext): void {
    // Precedence least -> most trusted (a field undefined at a given layer never overwrites a
    // value already set by a more trusted one - see xray-trace-header.ts doc comment for why):
    // per-call context (caller-supplied metadata) < baseContext < getContext() (AsyncLocalStorage,
    // never forgeable from outside the process) < xray* fields derived from the Lambda runtime env.
    const merged = mergeDefined(
      context,
      this.baseContext,
      getContext() ?? {},
      parseXrayTraceHeader(process.env["_X_AMZN_TRACE_ID"]) ?? {},
    );
    const redacted = this.redactor.redact(merged) as Record<string, unknown>;
    const line = JSON.stringify({
      timestamp: this.now(),
      level,
      event,
      ...redacted,
    });
    this.sink(level, line);
  }
}

export const logger = new SecureLogger();

/**
 * Request-scoped correlation context via AsyncLocalStorage (m5-observability-design.md #2).
 * runWithContext is called once per record for batch handlers (SQS, DynamoDB Streams, sweeper) -
 * never once per invocation for those - so context never leaks across records in the same batch.
 */
import { AsyncLocalStorage } from "node:async_hooks";

export interface RequestContext {
  correlationId: string;
  tenantId?: string;
  requestId?: string;
}

const storage = new AsyncLocalStorage<RequestContext>();

export function runWithContext<T>(context: RequestContext, fn: () => T): T {
  return storage.run(context, fn);
}

export function getContext(): RequestContext | undefined {
  return storage.getStore();
}

/**
 * m5-observability-design.md #2: correlationId for an SQS record whose sender (relay/
 * sweeper's composition-root senders, src/runtime/aws/composition/*.ts) propagates the
 * original business correlationId via this exact MessageAttribute name - see
 * outboxRecordCorrelationId (src/shared/outbox/outbox.ts) for the sender side of this same
 * contract. Falls back to the SQS messageId only for a message that never got the
 * attribute (pre-M5 in-flight, or a queue whose producer has no upstream correlationId to
 * propagate, e.g. SES/SNS-sourced messages) - pulled out as its own pure function so the
 * extraction itself is unit-testable without a handler module's side-effecting top-level
 * AWS client construction.
 */
export function correlationIdFromSqsRecord(record: { messageAttributes?: Record<string, { stringValue?: string }>; messageId: string }): string {
  return record.messageAttributes?.["correlationId"]?.stringValue ?? record.messageId;
}

E confirma que dimensões de alta cardinalidade (como `tenantId`, um user ID, um request ID) são o antipadrão nomeado explicitamente na doc — validando a restrição que `logger.ts` já tinha registrado por instinto de custo, agora com fonte externa citada.

docs/architecture/reviews/emf-metrics-dashboard-scoping\round1-codex-output.txt:2792:1. **BLOQUEANTE — Proposta §2, instrumentação nos três `src/workers/**`**: inserir `emitMetric()` diretamente nos workers viola a invariante arquitetural explícita de `AGENTS.md` §7: workers devem permanecer puros e observability-agnostic. A emissão deve ocorrer nos handlers Lambda, traduzindo os outcomes retornados, ou entrar como porta injetada. Isso é especialmente claro em `guest-credential-delivery/deliver.ts`, que injeta até o alerta justamente para permanecer AWS/runtime-agnostic.
docs/architecture/reviews/emf-metrics-dashboard-scoping\round1-codex-output.txt:2804:7. **BLOQUEANTE — Proposta §§1–2, garantia de não lançar**: `emitMetric()` é declarado como “nunca lança”, mas o pseudocódigo não mostra contenção de exceções nem sink injetável. Se essa for a garantia contratual, serialização e escrita precisam ficar dentro de `try/catch`, com teste que faça o sink lançar e prove que o processamento de negócio continua.

 696: #    isDeliveryRecord(), and inflate batchItemFailures/the failure queue with pure noise (a latent
 697: #    bug that predates D-233 too, since claim()'s original PutItem already wrote a marker row).
 698: #  - maximum_retry_attempts/maximum_record_age_in_seconds set to -1 (unlimited, bounded only by
 699: #    DynamoDB Streams' own native 24h retention) - a bounded retry count/age (the previous
 700: #    3/3600) could exhaust BEFORE the marker's 30s claim lease ever expires, leaving
 701: #    SKIPPED_LEASE_ACTIVE retries stuck with no path to the SEND_UNCERTAIN reconciliation
 702: #    deliver.ts relies on. The IteratorAge alarm below is the real backstop for a genuinely
 703: #    stuck/poison record - it fires in minutes, not after the 24h native retention discards it.

 734: # D-233: real-time backstop replacing the "Errors alarm gives visibility" assumption that turned
 735: # out to be false - ReportBatchItemFailures means a retried record never surfaces as a Lambda
 736: # Errors metric, the invocation returns normally. IteratorAge is the correct signal: a record
 737: # stuck retrying (SKIPPED_LEASE_ACTIVE/SEND_FAILED looping) delays the shard's cursor immediately,
 738: # well before the stream's native 24h retention would silently discard it.
 739: resource "aws_cloudwatch_metric_alarm" "guest_credential_delivery_iterator_age" {
 740:   alarm_name          = "${local.name_prefix}-guest-credential-delivery-iterator-age"
 741:   namespace           = "AWS/Lambda"
 742:   metric_name         = "IteratorAge"
 743:   dimensions          = { FunctionName = module.guest_credential_delivery_handler.function_name }
 744:   statistic           = "Maximum"
 745:   period              = 300
 746:   evaluation_periods  = 1
 747:   threshold           = 300000 # 5 minutes in ms - far below the stream's 24h native retention.
 748:   comparison_operator = "GreaterThanThreshold"
 749:   alarm_description   = "guest-credential-delivery: stream cursor is falling behind - a record is being retried repeatedly (SKIPPED_LEASE_ACTIVE/SEND_FAILED) without converging to SENT/SEND_UNCERTAIN. Investigate before the stream's native 24h retention silently discards it."
 750:   treat_missing_data  = "notBreaching"
 751:   alarm_actions       = [module.alert_topic.topic_arn]
 752:   tags                = { Project = local.project_name, Environment = var.environment }
 753: }

NOTA: 7,2/10  
VEREDITO: NEEDS FIXES  
ACHADOS:

1. **BLOQUEANTE — Achado #5 não fechado:** `dispatch-outbox-relay-processor.ts:55` chama `runWithContext({ correlationId })` sem `tenantId`; o contexto interno em `relay.ts:74` também contém somente `correlationId`. Logo, o log de outcome em `dispatch-outbox-relay-processor.ts:63` não possui `tenantId`, e a consulta Logs Insights proposta não entrega visão por tenant para esse pipeline. Deve incluir `tenantId: item.tenantId` no contexto do handler.

2. **BLOQUEANTE — Achado #4 continua factualmente impreciso:** a fila e o alarme existem, mas não representam imediatamente o agregado de `SEND_FAILED`/`SKIPPED_LEASE_ACTIVE`. Esses outcomes retornam partial batch failure e são retentados indefinidamente; o sinal imediato existente é o alarme de `IteratorAge` em `infra/main.tf:739-751`. `notifyUncertainDelivery` alimenta a fila nos estados incertos, enquanto `on_failure` só a recebe quando o event source mapping finalmente abandona o registro. A revisão precisa descrever esses dois mecanismos separadamente.

3. **BLOQUEANTE — Dashboard omite o sinal nativo correto do guest worker:** o próprio código documenta em `infra/main.tf:734-738` que `AWS/Lambda Errors` não registra falhas reportadas por `ReportBatchItemFailures`. Portanto, o widget “Lambda saúde” não detectará `SEND_FAILED`/`SKIPPED_LEASE_ACTIVE`; o dashboard deve incluir `IteratorAge` da função guest, preferencialmente com o alarme existente.

4. **BLOQUEANTE — exceções não viram outcomes observáveis em dois pipelines:** somente o reminder especifica `HANDLER_ERROR`. As exceções capturadas em `dispatch-outbox-relay-processor.ts:67-70` e `guest-credential-delivery-handler.ts:63-66` não emitem métrica e seus logs não possuem o campo `outcome`, ficando ausentes tanto dos widgets customizados quanto da query proposta. É necessário definir `HANDLER_ERROR` para os três handlers ou ampliar explicitamente a consulta para os eventos de erro.

5. **NÃO FECHADO — Achado #6 contém Terraform inválido:** `dashboard_name` não pode ter default `"${name_prefix}-operations"` referenciando outra variável. Use `default = null` e um `local.dashboard_name`, ou derive o nome exclusivamente em `main.tf`.

6. **NÃO FECHADO — quantidade de log groups inconsistente:** são quatro funções/log groups no escopo — reminder, relay de e-mail, relay de WhatsApp e guest credential — embora a revisão declare “os 3 log groups”. O processor do relay é compartilhado por duas Lambdas distintas; o wiring e as queries devem nomear os quatro grupos sem ambiguidade.

Confirmados como fechados: instrumentação restrita aos handlers; cinco outcomes de reminder; cinco de relay; nove de guest; nomenclatura de publicação; contrato non-throwing testável; limites e custos EMF corrigidos; distinção entre roteamento e entrega final explicitada.
